| 3 | **`runtimeClassName` (gVisor): supported, off by default, measured before anyone turns it on.** The mechanism already exists (`pod_config.ex:70,276-286`, `pod_spec.ex:602`); what is missing is the readiness row, the cost measurement (a spike, H9–H11) and the shared-kernel line in the doctor. | On by default (breaks hosts without `runsc`, restricts scheduling to runsc nodes and is unmeasured on build workloads); required on shared clusters (a fail-closed `degraded: shared_kernel` is the alternative). | **OD1** |
| 4 | **Worker pods keep zero direct egress; the "egress allowlist" lands where it can be enforced.** Model APIs and git hosts are reached through the bridges and the primary's own `Egress` policy and audit (K§9.3); adding them to the pod NetworkPolicy would *bypass* that policy. The allowlist is the **controller's** egress (primary, tailscale control plane, API server) and is narrowed from today's `0.0.0.0/0:443`. The registry is not in any pod policy: the kubelet pulls, and kubelet traffic is not subject to NetworkPolicy. | FQDN allowlists on worker pods; model-API/git CIDRs in the worker policy. | OD8 |
| 5 | **An `arbiter-node` Helm chart is the primary install path**, published as an OCI artifact to GHCR by the release workflow, chart `version` = `appVersion` = the release. The raw manifests (`GET /nodes/join/k8s.yaml`) stay as the fallback and a **parity test** keeps the two from drifting. | Manifests only (K§0 decision 3); Kustomize; an operator/CRD. | OD9 |
| 6 | **One upgrade owner: for a chart install Helm owns the controller Deployment; K9 self-upgrade is disabled** (`rbac.selfUpgrade` false, no `patch` verb) and the node reports `upgrade available: helm upgrade …`. Raw-manifest installs keep K9 self-upgrade. | Controller owns (`lookup` hacks make `helm upgrade` fight the live image); both own (silent drift, `helm rollback` that rolls back nothing); the primary running `helm` (a cluster credential on the primary). | **OD2** |
| 7 | **Secrets never ride `--set`.** The join token comes from a pre-created Secret (`kubectl create secret … --from-file=token=/dev/stdin`) or, as a fallback, `--set-file`; the tailscale auth key and pull secrets are referenced by name only. | `--set join.token=…` (shell history, `ps`, Helm release Secret). | — |
| 8 | **Cluster-admin vs namespace-admin split follows the existing bootstrap/node split** (`install_manifest.ex:268-285`): the guardrails (Namespace + PSA labels, PriorityClass, ResourceQuota, LimitRange, NetworkPolicies, the optional admission policy and RuntimeClass) are cluster-admin's; the controller, its RBAC and config are a namespace admin's. The chart has `mode: all \| bootstrap \| node`, `all` by default. | One undifferentiated install; two charts. | **OD5** |
| 9 | **Controller image: built and pushed to GHCR (public) by the release workflow**; the chart's default. `nodes.registry` is no longer needed to *install* a node. | Primary-pushed `<nodes.registry>/controller` (today, `pipeline.ex:258-292`). | **OD6** |
| 10 | **Worker images are per repo: a `worker.repos.<repo>.image` block with `source: containerfile \| generated \| prebuilt`, its own `registry` and a credential reference**, never one `<registry>/worker` for everything (today `pipeline.ex:90,138,175`). Each cluster node lists the registry prefixes it holds pull secrets for, and each pod references only the secret for its own image. | The install-wide `nodes.registry` + one `image_pull_secrets` list for all pods (`pod_config.ex:72`, `pod_spec.ex:285-289`); a per-workspace registry only. | **OD3** |
| 11 | **Builds: the primary builds `containerfile`/`generated` and overlays the CLI and seed layers; `prebuilt` is built by the repo's CI** (from a published Arbiter runtime base) and the primary only overlays; **in-cluster BuildKit is rejected for v1.** | In-cluster BuildKit (needs privileges the PSA-restricted namespace forbids and a push credential in the cluster, K§11); primary-only. | **OD4** |

### 20.1 What exists today (the code this section changes)

| Area | Today | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| Install render | Two parts. **bootstrap** = Namespace (PSA `restricted` enforce/audit/warn), PriorityClass `arbiter-worker` (`-100`, `Never`), ResourceQuota, LimitRange, four NetworkPolicies, optional ValidatingAdmissionPolicy; **node** = two ServiceAccounts, Role + RoleBinding, placeholder Secrets, ConfigMaps, Lease, Service, Deployment. | `install_manifest.ex:257-286`, `:330-354`, `:358-382`, `:387-408`, `:413-468`, `:504-525` |
| Form fields | `name namespace max cpu memory node_selector pull_secret reach admission self_upgrade api_cidrs cluster_cidrs part`; `reach` is `direct \| tailscale` only. | `install_manifest.ex:98-113`, `:211`; `cluster_install.ex:22` |
| Spread / affinity | **None.** The worker pod has `nodeSelector`, `tolerations`, `runtimeClassName` (all optional) and no `topologySpreadConstraints`. The scheduler's *default* spreading does not help: it only applies to pods selected by a Service/ReplicaSet/StatefulSet, and worker pods are bare Pods labelled `…/component: worker`, which the controller's Service does not select. | `pod_spec.ex:578-603` |
| Placement config | `placement: {node_selector, tolerations, priority_class, runtime_class}`; defaults are `{}` / `[]` / `arbiter-worker` / `""`. So the *code* has no dedicated-node default; the assumption lives in docs: §4.1's example ConfigMap, §13's conditions (2) and (4), `docs/remote-workers-k8s-runbook.md:187-190` (step 6) and `:205`. | `pod_config.ex:68-71`, `:272-291` |
| Hardening | `hostUsers: false` is **hard-coded** (not a config key); PSA `restricted` on the namespace; `seccompProfile: RuntimeDefault`; non-root asserted by the builder and the admission policy because PSA stops checking under `hostUsers: false` (K1-A1). | `pod_spec.ex:580`, `pod_security.ex:120`, `admission_policy.ex:99-104` |
| Network policy | `default-deny` (both directions); workers → controller 9443/9444 only, **no DNS**; controller → API server, kube-dns, and **`0.0.0.0/0` minus the cluster CIDRs on TCP 443, UDP 41641, UDP 3478**. | `install_manifest.ex:422-467` (the wide rule at `:457-464`) |
| Reach | `direct` (controller dials `ARB_PRIMARY_URL`) or `tailscale` (userspace sidecar, proxy `127.0.0.1:1055`, `ARB_NODE_PROXY`); the sidecar image is the floating tag `ghcr.io/tailscale/tailscale:stable`. The controller already honours `ARB_NODE_PROXY` for any proxy. | `controller_manifest.ex:24,130-133,137-174` |
| Join / Add node | The modal/CLI show `kubectl apply -f <(curl -fsSL "<public_url>/nodes/join/k8s.yaml?…")` and `read -rs T && printf %s "$T" \| kubectl -n NS create secret generic arbiter-join --from-file=token=/dev/stdin`. The route answers 503 until `nodes.public_url`, **`nodes.registry`** and a release exist. | `cluster_install.ex:49-57,107-129`; `apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_controller.ex:66-100` |
| Upgrade (K9) | The Role gets `patch` on `deployments/arbiter-controller` when `self_upgrade` (default on); the controller patches its `controller` container to an image of **the repository it already runs** when idle; an `outdated` node that cannot patch is shown `kubectl set image …`. | `install_manifest.ex:505,522`; `self_upgrade.ex:42,54-59,70-81,92-93`; `overview.ex:195-220`; `cluster_install.ex:136` |
| Controller image | Built **on the primary** from the retained release tarball (`Containerfile`: debian trixie-slim + libs + `ADD release.tar.gz`, uid 10001) and pushed to `<nodes.registry>/controller:<version>` by a boot task after each server start. | `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:258-307`; `worker/image/publisher.ex:125`; `nodes/cluster_install.ex:79` |
| Worker image | `Image.plan/3` reads `.tool-versions` and `.arbiter/Containerfile` **from the default branch's committed tree** (never `HEAD`, never a worktree); with a Containerfile the image is `repo-<slug>`, without it a `beam-<elixir>-<erlang>[-node<N>]` image is generated. Layers: toolchain → CLI (`claude`, `arb`) → seed (`DepsCache`), all pushed to **`<nodes.registry>/worker`**, the run image pinned by digest. There is no `prebuilt` source. | `worker/image.ex:186-230`, `:264-277`; `pipeline.ex:86-93` (`push(plan.tag, "worker", …)` at `:90`, `:138`, `:175`) |
| Registry | One install-wide registry and one credential: settings `nodes.registry`, `nodes.registry_username`, `nodes.registry_password` (Cloak, write-only), `nodes.registry_insecure`, all operator-only. | `worker/image/registry.ex:36-57`; `settings/registry.ex:148-175` |
| Registry in the cluster | `PodConfig` requires one `registry` and one flat `image_pull_secrets` list (every pod references all of them); `PodSpec.image/2` accepts a ref only if it starts with `cfg.registry <> "/"`; the admission policy admits `<registry>/`, `docker.io/library/` and `service_image_allowlist` prefixes. | `pod_config.ex:37,72,101`; `pod_spec.ex:285-289`; `admission_policy.ex:59-66` |
| Per-repo config | `worker.repos.<repo>` deep-merges over `worker.*` and today carries `seed_paths`, `prepush_*`, `pre_push_*`, `max_concurrent`; no image keys. Credentials are *workspace secrets* (`arb workspace secret set`), referenced as `secret:<key>`. | `tasks/workspace/config_schema.ex:366-420`, `:122`, `:533-535`; `docs/git-credentials.md:30-31` |
| Release | `v*` tag → ubi8 build → tarball + checksum → GitHub Release (`softprops/action-gh-release`). Nothing is pushed to GHCR; no chart exists. `ARB_BUILD_RELEASE_REPO=${{ github.repository }}` is stamped into the release. | `.github/workflows/release.yml:31,173,206` |

### 20.2 A. Spread across the cluster, and hard bounds

**Why spread is not free on a shared cluster.** Putting workers on every node means putting untrusted, CPU- and memory-hungry LLM-driven builds next to vstim prod on every node. Spread is only acceptable because the cluster, not the controller, bounds what a worker can take. The ring-fence is therefore the heart of this section; spread is one line.

**Spread.** `PodSpec.build/2` adds to every worker pod (and the canary, which uses the same builder, `canary.ex`):

```yaml
topologySpreadConstraints:
- maxSkew: 1
  topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
  whenUnsatisfiable: ScheduleAnyway          # OD7
  nodeTaintsPolicy: Honor                    # a node the pod cannot tolerate is not a "domain" [H1]
  nodeAffinityPolicy: Honor
  labelSelector: {matchLabels: {app.kubernetes.io/component: worker, arbiter.dev/node: <node-id>}}
```

* The selector includes `arbiter.dev/node` so two installs in one cluster do not spread against each other. `arbiter.dev/run` is *not* in it (it differs per pod).
* `ScheduleAnyway` is the default because a hard constraint on a small, uneven cluster wedges: with two nodes of 8 and 4 CPUs, `DoNotSchedule` leaves a pod `Pending` whenever the small node is full even though the big node has room, and `Pending` is exactly the state the controller turns into `refuse{unschedulable}` after `schedule_s` (K§4.2). Soft spread still moves the median pod to the emptier node. OD7 lists the alternative.
* `placement.spread` joins the closed ConfigMap schema (`pod_config.ex:272-291`): `{enabled: true, topology_keys: [kubernetes.io/hostname], max_skew: 1, when_unsatisfiable: ScheduleAnyway}`. Zone spread is `topology_keys: [kubernetes.io/hostname, topology.kubernetes.io/zone]`; a key the nodes do not carry is a `bad_config`, not a silent no-op. The controller Deployment itself (one replica, `Recreate`) gets no spread.
* **Remove the dedicated-node assumption.** Code needs no change for the default (`node_selector` is already `{}`). Docs do: §4.1's example (done above), §13 conditions (2) and (4) (marked), runbook step 6 and the closing remark of step 9 (a ticket, §20.14 KH15). `node_selector` and `tolerations` stay as options for operators who do want a pool; the chart exposes them as `placement.nodeSelector` / `placement.tolerations`.

**Hard per-pod bounds** (what the cluster enforces whatever the controller emits):

| Bound | Today | Proposed |
|---|---|---|
| `PriorityClass arbiter-worker` | `-100`, `preemptionPolicy: Never`, not global default (`install_manifest.ex:344-354`) | **unchanged.** Consequence worth stating: under node pressure the kubelet evicts among pods that exceed their requests by priority, so a worker (−100) goes before a prod pod of default priority 0 that is also over its request. It does not protect a prod pod from a *noisy neighbour on CPU* (CPU is throttled by limits, not evicted): the CPU limit is what protects it. |
| `ResourceQuota` | `pods`, `requests.cpu/memory`, `limits.cpu/memory`, `requests.ephemeral-storage`, derived from `max_concurrent` and the per-pod figures (`install_manifest.ex:358-382`) | add **`limits.ephemeral-storage`**; keep deriving from `max` (see §20.5 for how the chart gets the numbers). |
| `LimitRange` | `defaultRequest`/`default` only (`install_manifest.ex:387-408`): a pod may still *ask* for 64 CPUs | add **`max`** (cpu = 2× the worker limit, memory = the worker limit, ephemeral-storage = `work_size_limit` + `tmp_size_limit`), **`maxLimitRequestRatio`** (cpu 4, memory 2), and a `Pod`-type `max`, so a builder bug or a hostile `service_image_allowlist` entry cannot exceed the shape the operator set. |
| Memory burst | worker `limits.memory` = 2 × `requests.memory` (`install_manifest.ex:597`) | unchanged by default (2 Gi request / 4 Gi limit is what a Mix test run needs to compile), but now **capped by `LimitRange`** and visible as a chart value; OD7 asks whether a shared cluster should set limit = request. |
| Ephemeral storage | the `work` `emptyDir` has `sizeLimit` 8 Gi (kubelet eviction), a *request* of 4 Gi, no limit | add a container `limits.ephemeral-storage` equal to `work_size_limit + tmp`, so the runaway write is bounded by the container limit and the quota, not only by an eviction tick (K1-A7: eviction is ~40-50 s after the limit is crossed). |
| PIDs | none | **cannot be set per pod from a manifest**; `podPidsLimit` is a kubelet setting. A fork bomb in a shared kernel is a *node* problem; this is one of the arguments for §20.3, and the doctor lists "kubelet `podPidsLimit` unknown" as an informational row. [H8] |

**Kept as built, listed so nobody re-opens them:** PSA `restricted` (enforce + audit + warn, `enforce-version: latest`, `install_manifest.ex:330-342`); `hostUsers: false` (`pod_spec.ex:580`; verified on k3s v1.36.5, K1; **not a chart value**: with `hostUsers: true` the in-container uid 10001 is the node's real uid 10001 and may own files there, so `false` is the isolation, and a cluster that cannot do user namespaces is unsupported rather than silently degraded, the canary and PSA dry-run say so); `automountServiceAccountToken: false`; `seccomp RuntimeDefault`; no `appArmorProfile` (K1-A2); `dnsPolicy: None`; `enableServiceLinks: false`.

**KH2 as built (bd-3rdz1h).** `placement.spread` renders one `topologySpreadConstraints` entry per `topology_keys` (default `kubernetes.io/hostname`, `maxSkew` 1, `ScheduleAnyway`, `nodeTaintsPolicy` and `nodeAffinityPolicy` `Honor`, selector `component: worker` + `arbiter.dev/node`); `enabled: false` omits it. Key syntax is validated, but whether the nodes carry a key is not (that needs a cluster read). The worker container's `limits.ephemeral-storage` defaults to `work_size_limit + tmp_size_limit` (9Gi). The `LimitRange` `Container` entry gains `max` (cpu 2× the worker limit, memory the worker limit, ephemeral-storage the work + tmp figure, each raised to the controller's own limit if that is larger) and `maxLimitRequestRatio` (cpu 4, memory 2); the `Pod` entry's `max` is the effective pod (`Quota.demand/1`: worker + `snapshotter` sidecar) plus four run services at `services_resources`. To satisfy the ratio every container states a cpu/memory pair within it: the `LimitRange` defaults are now request 100m / 128Mi / 128Mi eph and limit 400m / 256Mi / 1Gi eph, `services_resources` states cpu 400m and eph 128Mi/1Gi explicitly, and the controller and tailscale sidecar requests rose (cpu 250m / 125m, tailscale memory 128Mi). The ephemeral-storage default limit is what keeps the `max` from becoming every sidecar's limit. The quota gains `limits.ephemeral-storage`. `limit_range_admission_test.exs` builds a real pod and re-implements the `LimitRanger` checks (container ratio and max, `Pod` max) and the quota fit at `max_concurrent`. KH0's k3d run (v1.35.5) refuted H14 as first written (the old defaults broke the cpu 4 / memory 2 ratio on `seed` and `snapshotter`; a `Pod` max equal to the worker limit refused the pod because native sidecars count; an ephemeral `max` became every container's limit), and the figures above are the fix; the test here reproduces those three refusals in simulation, it was not re-run on a live cluster.

**PSA must fail closed.** The `psa` readiness check already exists (`node_agent/k8s/readiness.ex:14,165-200`, K13: a server-side dry run of the builder's pod must pass and the same pod with `privileged` must be refused) but is a doctor `[warn]`, while a netpol failure degrades the node (`readiness_monitor.ex:56,255-264`). On a shared cluster an unlabelled namespace is the likelier mistake (§20.6 makes the label a cluster-admin step), so a failed `psa` becomes `degraded: psa_unenforced`, excluded from placement like `netpol_unenforced`, with the same audited override. (KH4.)

### 20.3 A. `runtimeClassName` / gVisor: on or off by default

**What exists.** `placement.runtime_class` → `runtimeClassName` on the pod (`pod_config.ex:70,276-286`, `pod_spec.ex:601`), default empty ("set to gvisor/kata where it exists", K§4.1). Nothing checks that the class exists or what it costs.

**Why it matters here.** §13 says it plainly: on runc the isolation is user namespace + seccomp + dropped capabilities + NetworkPolicy, "a notch below the podman path", and a worker pod shares a **kernel** with vstim prod and CI. Everything above the kernel is now bounded by the hardening of §20.2; the kernel attack surface is not. Two honest aspects: user namespaces, which §8 relies on, *widen* the unprivileged kernel surface a little (more code reachable without privilege) while shrinking the blast radius of a container-root escape; and a kernel bug reached by LLM-driven code is the one threat none of the other controls address.

**What gVisor (`runsc`) would change.** A user-space kernel (the Sentry) answers the pod's syscalls, so the host kernel sees a small, fixed set instead of the whole table; the gofer mediates file access. It is the only option on the table that removes the shared-kernel premise without a separate machine.

**Cost (expected; none of it measured by this ticket, [H9–H11]).**

| Axis | Expectation | Why it matters for Arbiter |
|---|---|---|
| CPU/syscall-heavy and file-heavy work | slower, worst on many-small-file I/O and process spawning | a worker's whole job is `git`, `mix deps.compile`, `mix test`, `npm`: thousands of files and short processes. The seed layer exists to avoid exactly that cost. Benchmark: `mix deps.compile && mix test` for arbiter and vstim, runc vs runsc on the same node, wall time and CPU. |
| Memory-backed `emptyDir` | works; `/tmp` and `/run/arb` are `emptyDir{medium: Memory}` | confirm the 80 %-of-`sizeLimit` snapshotter poll (K1-A7) still sees correct usage. |
| `hostUsers: false` + runsc | **not supported** (KH1, H10): runsc refuses it, and every pod stays in `ContainerCreating` with `can't set spec.hostUsers: false, RuntimeClass handler "runsc" does not support user namespaces`. A dry-run create cannot see this (the kubelet fails the sandbox, not the API server) | runsc *replaces* the user namespace with its sandbox, so under a runsc-handled class the builder **omits `hostUsers`** (`pod_spec.ex:610`), and the admission policy (`admission_policy.ex:106-108`) and `pod_security.ex:123,425` accept an absent `hostUsers` for that class only. Every other runtime still requires `hostUsers: false`. The class is marked by `placement.runtime_handler: runsc` (`pod_config.ex:101`), because the cluster-scoped `RuntimeClass` is not readable by the controller's Role. |
| Guard mounts | the K1-A8 `subPath` mounts of `.git`, `config`, `hooks`, `commondir`, `alternates` must still fail `mv`/`rm` with `EBUSY`/`EROFS` | **re-run K8 under runsc**; if the sandbox implements mounts differently the tamper guarantees (bd-6t7u81) are void. [H11] |
| Sidecars, `tini`, `socat OPENSSL`, Postgres | expected to work; Postgres under runsc has needed care with shared memory | rerun the K10/K3 probes. |
| Scheduling | a `RuntimeClass` with `scheduling.nodeSelector` pins pods to nodes that have `runsc` | **conflicts with "spread across the whole cluster"**: gVisor mode means "spread across the runsc nodes". The chart must say so. |
| Install | `runsc` + containerd shim + a containerd runtime stanza **on every node** (k3s: a `config.toml.tmpl`), then a cluster-scoped `RuntimeClass` | node-level, cluster-admin, and not something a chart can do; the chart can render the `RuntimeClass` object only (`runtimeClass.create`, default false). |
| Network | NetworkPolicy still applies at the pod's veth, but runsc uses its own network stack | rerun K2 and the seed gate under runsc. |

**Options.**

* **(a) Off by default, supported, measured (recommended).** Chart value `placement.runtimeClassName: ""`. The readiness block reports `runtime_class: "" (runc, shared kernel)` or the class name and whether the `RuntimeClass` exists (the Role cannot read cluster-scoped objects, so as with `priority_class` the check is the server-side dry run's verdict, `remote-workers-k8s-runbook.md` §3), and the doctor prints a **shared-kernel line** (the controller cannot read `nodes` or other namespaces' pods, so the line states the fact, "workers share the node kernel with other workloads", and no count). The mesaana install flips to a class only after the spike gives numbers the operator accepts.
* (b) On by default where a `RuntimeClass` named `gvisor` exists. Auto-detection means behaviour changes when someone installs a class; reject.
* (c) Required on shared clusters (a chart value `sharedCluster: true` that fails the install without `runtimeClassName`, or a `degraded: shared_kernel` unless acknowledged). The strongest posture and the one to take if the spike shows the cost is tolerable; until then it would make the first target (a runc-only k3s) uninstallable, which §13 has just ruled out.

**Recommendation: (a)**, with KH4 (readiness row, doctor line, chart value) and KH0 (the spike H9-H11) filed together, and a stated trigger for (c): if the spike shows ≤ the operator's acceptable slowdown on arbiter's and vstim's test suites and the guard mounts hold, the operator decides whether to make the sandboxed runtime mandatory on mesaana. **OD1.**

### 20.4 A. Network: default-deny and an egress allowlist that exists

The operator asked for "default-deny with an egress allowlist (model APIs, git hosts, registry, primary only)". Reading the policies against how traffic actually flows (K§9) changes what the allowlist is:

* **Workers.** `default-deny` plus `worker-to-controller` (TCP 9443/9444, no DNS; `install_manifest.ex:423-428`) already gives a worker **no egress other than the controller**. Everything a worker sends to a model API, a git host or the Arbiter MCP goes through a bridge into the primary's `Egress` listener, where the primary's allowlist, `egress_events` audit and `BridgeIdentity` apply (K§9.3). If the pod policy also allowed the model-API and git-host CIDRs, a worker could reach them *directly*, outside the primary's policy and audit, with no DNS and an unauditable destination set (CIDRs of CDN-fronted hosts change). So the answer is **not** to add those to the worker policy. The allowlist the operator wants *is* the primary's egress policy; the doctor should print it next to the cluster readiness so the two are read together. This is the property K§9.3 was designed to keep.
* **Registry.** Image pulls are done by the **kubelet on the node**; node-originated traffic is outside NetworkPolicy. No pod policy needs a registry rule, and adding one would suggest a guarantee that does not exist. The registries matter to (1) the admission policy's image allowlist and (2) the pull secrets, §20.10.
* **Controller.** This is where the allowlist is real and currently wide: `0.0.0.0/0` minus the cluster CIDRs on TCP 443, UDP 41641, UDP 3478 (`install_manifest.ex:457-464`), so a compromised controller can reach any HTTPS host. The controller needs: the API server (already pinned by `api_cidrs`), kube-dns (already pinned), and **the primary**. For `reach: direct` that is the primary's address(es): chart value `networkPolicy.controllerEgress.primaryCIDRs` (required; `/32`s or the reverse proxy's range), replacing the `0.0.0.0/0` rule. For `reach: tailscale` the destinations are the tailnet control plane and DERP relays, which are hostnames with changing IPs: the wide rule must stay (443 + UDP 41641/3478) unless the CNI has FQDN policy (Cilium `toFQDNs`, Calico DNS policy). Chart `networkPolicy.flavor: kubernetes | cilium | calico` selects an FQDN-capable rendering later; v1 ships `kubernetes` and documents the residual. For `reach: proxy` the egress is the proxy's address only.
* **A new `reach: proxy`** (chart value `reach.mode=proxy`, `reach.proxy.url`): the controller already reads `ARB_NODE_PROXY` (`controller_manifest.ex:130-133`); the chart sets it and narrows egress to the proxy. This covers "reach (tailscale/proxy)" without a sidecar.
* **Gap to close:** the canary probes `api`, `controller_port`, `foreign`, `internet`, `node` (K13, `canary.ex:40-43,83`); add the **cloud metadata address** (`169.254.169.254`) as a probe, which a default-deny blocks but nothing proves (KH3). Pin the tailscale sidecar to a version instead of `:stable` (`controller_manifest.ex:24`): a floating tag on a pod that holds a standing auth key.
* **Enforcement stays fail-closed** (K§9.4): the chart has no value that removes the policies; a CNI that does not enforce makes the node `degraded: netpol_unenforced` and the seed gate refuses the pod (K1-A3).

OD8 records the alternative (FQDN policies on workers) and why it is not recommended.

**KH3 as built (bd-21l4hk).** `InstallManifest` takes `primary_cidrs` (`direct`; required, a `/0` is refused; the rule's port is the primary URL's), and `proxy_url` + `proxy_cidrs` (`reach=proxy`; required; the proxy's port only, `ARB_NODE_PROXY` set to the URL, no sidecar, a URL with credentials or a path refused). `reach=tailscale` keeps the `0.0.0.0/0` minus cluster CIDRs rule (443, UDP 41641/3478): it is the one case where the controller still has wide egress, and the acceptance line "no `0.0.0.0/0`" holds for `direct` and `proxy` only. The tailscale sidecar is `ghcr.io/tailscale/tailscale:v1.104.1@sha256:87c55d6b…` (tag and digest read from ghcr.io on 2026-10-16; bump both together). The canary gains `metadata` (`169.254.169.254:80`): mandatory, and a connect is `unenforced`. The Helm values (`networkPolicy.controllerEgress`, `reach.mode`) are KH5's.

### 20.5 B. The `arbiter-node` Helm chart

**Why K§0 decision 3 is reversed.** K§2.1 rejected Helm for three reasons: a second versioned artifact, hosting, and `genCA` regenerating on upgrade. Re-weighed for shared clusters with more nodes coming: *hosting* is solved by an OCI chart in GHCR next to the controller image; *skew* is solved by cutting the chart in the same release workflow with `version == appVersion == <release>` (a chart cannot be older or newer than the controller it installs, and the primary already knows its release tag, `nodes/cluster_install.ex:53`); *`genCA`* does not arise because the chart has no certificates (the controller makes its CA at runtime into a Secret, K§9.2). What the plain manifests cannot give is the lifecycle: `helm upgrade` / `rollback` / `uninstall`, `helm diff` and GitOps controllers (Flux, Argo) for an operator who keeps the cluster in git, and a values file instead of 13 query parameters that keep growing (`install_manifest.ex:98-113`). The raw manifests stay as the fallback (the ticket asks for it) and for clusters without Helm.

**Layout** (`charts/arbiter-node/`, a new directory in this repo):

```
Chart.yaml        name: arbiter-node, version == appVersion == the release (v0.2.31 -> 0.2.31), kubeVersion ">=1.30.0-0" [H12]
values.yaml
values.schema.json
templates/bootstrap/  priorityclass  resourcequota  limitrange  networkpolicies  admissionpolicy (optional)  runtimeclass (optional)
templates/node/       serviceaccounts  role  rolebinding  placeholder secrets/configmaps  lease  service  deployment  [join secret only for --set-file]
templates/NOTES.txt   next steps: create the join Secret, `kubectl -n NS logs deploy/arbiter-controller`, `arb node show NAME`
```

`mode: all | bootstrap | node` (default `all`) is exactly `?part=` of `GET /nodes/join/k8s.yaml` (`install_manifest.ex:226-229`, `:288-299`), so the two renderers have one vocabulary. **No `Namespace` template** (§20.6). **Object names are fixed**, not prefixed with the release name: the Role's `resourceNames`, the Service, the Lease, the ownerReference of every worker pod (`pod_spec.ex:563-569`), the NetworkPolicy selectors and `SelfUpgrade`'s constants (`self_upgrade.ex:30-31`) all name `arbiter-controller` and `arbiter-worker`. Consequence, stated in `NOTES.txt` and the schema: **one release per namespace**, and the release name is the node name (≤ 53 characters; the node-name rule is already 63, `install_manifest.ex:61,165`).

**Values** (the first column is today's form field, the second its chart value; everything new is below the line):

| Today (`install_manifest.ex:98-113`) | Chart value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | `node.name` | required; `ARB_NODE_NAME` and the release name |
| `namespace` | `--namespace` | not a value; the chart renders into `.Release.Namespace` |
| `max` | `concurrency.max` | `max_concurrent` in the ConfigMap (`install_manifest.ex:584-604`) |
| `cpu`, `memory` | `workers.requests.cpu` / `.memory`, `workers.limits.*` | limits default to 2× as today (`:597`) |
| `node_selector` | `placement.nodeSelector` | empty by default (§20.2) |
| `pull_secret` | `registries[].pullSecret` (+ `imagePullSecrets` for the controller image) | per registry, §20.10 |
| `reach` | `reach.mode: direct \| tailscale \| proxy` | `reach.tailscale.{authKeySecret,image.tag}`, `reach.proxy.url`; no value carries a key |
| `admission` | `admissionPolicy.enabled` | needs Kubernetes ≥ 1.30 |
| `self_upgrade` | **removed**: always off in the chart | §20.7; the Role has no `patch` |
| `api_cidrs`, `cluster_cidrs` | `networkPolicy.apiServerCIDRs`, `.clusterCIDRs` | |
| `part` | `mode` | |
| *new* | `primary.url` | `ARB_PRIMARY_URL`, today supplied by the server |
| *new* | `image.{repository,tag,digest,pullPolicy}`, `imagePullSecrets` | default `ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-controller`, tag = `appVersion`; `digest` wins when set |
| *new* | `join.existingSecret` (default `arbiter-join`), `join.token` | `join.token` is for `--set-file` only; renders the Secret |
| *new* | `placement.{tolerations,spread.*,runtimeClassName,priorityClass.{create,name,value}}` | §20.2, §20.3 |
| *new* | `quota.hard`, `limitRange.*` | §20.2; numbers explicit, see below |
| *new* | `networkPolicy.controllerEgress.{primaryCIDRs,extra}`, `.flavor` | §20.4 |
| *new* | `registries[]`: `{prefix, pullSecret}` | §20.10 |
| *new* | `workers.{workSizeLimit,tmpSizeLimit,servicesResources,timeouts,snapshotIntervalS,serviceImageAllowlist}`, `controller.resources` | the rest of the closed ConfigMap schema (`pod_config.ex:54-76`) |

**Deliberately not values:** anything the pod builder owns (K§4.1 "Not settable, ever": security context, volumes, service account, `hostUsers`, host namespaces), `seccompProfile`, the PSA level, whether NetworkPolicies exist, the name of any object. A `values.schema.json` rejects unknown keys, so a typo is an install error, not a silent default.

**The join token and every other secret.** Three rules, enforced by the schema where a schema can and by `NOTES.txt` where it cannot:

1. The recommended path is a **pre-created Secret** `arbiter-join` (key `token`) made by `read -rs T && printf %s "$T" | kubectl -n NS create secret generic arbiter-join --from-file=token=/dev/stdin` (the same command `cluster_install.ex:126-129` builds today). The Deployment already mounts it `optional: true` (`controller_manifest.ex:181`), so the chart never needs to see it.
2. `--set-file join.token=./token` is supported as a fallback for automation; the chart then renders the Secret. **It is not free**: the rendered manifest, token included, is stored in Helm's release record (`sh.helm.release.v1.<name>.v<N>`, a Secret holding the gzipped manifest) until the release is deleted. The token is single-use and expires in 15 minutes (§5.1), so after first boot the stored copy is inert, but it is the reason this is the fallback and not the path. `--set join.token=…` is never shown anywhere (shell history, `ps`, `helm get values`).
3. The tailscale auth key and every pull secret are **names**, never values (`reach.tailscale.authKeySecret`, `registries[].pullSecret`, `imagePullSecrets`).

**The numbers in the quota.** Helm templates have integer arithmetic but no Kubernetes quantity parsing (`500m`, `4Gi`), and `InstallManifest.quota/1` (`install_manifest.ex:358-382`) derives the hard limits from `max` and the per-pod figures with `Quantity`. Re-implementing that in Go templates would be a second copy of the sizing rules. Recommended: `quota.hard` is an **explicit map in `values.yaml`** with the numbers for `max: 2` (the shipped default), and the primary renders a **values file** with the numbers computed by the Elixir code the manifests already use: `GET /nodes/join/values.yaml?name=…&max=…` (anonymous, same parser and 422 semantics as `k8s.yaml`, no secret by construction), `arb node add --kind cluster --format values`. An operator who installs without the primary edits the map by hand; the controller's headroom number and the `quota` readiness check already show a mismatch (K§4.3 last sentence). The alternative, restricting the chart to integer-valued `cpuMilli` / `memoryMi` and doing the arithmetic in sprig, keeps the chart standalone at the price of a second copy of the formula and an awkward interface; not recommended.

**What Helm must not own.** The controller writes `arbiter-node-credential`, `arbiter-controller-ca` and `arbiter-ca` (the RBAC grants `update` on those names only) and the Lease's holder (`install_manifest.ex:516-521`). Helm 3's three-way merge preserves a live field that neither the old nor the new manifest declares, so rendering these as empty placeholders should survive `helm upgrade`; `helm upgrade --force` (replace) would not. This is **[H2]** and the first thing the spike verifies. Fallback if it fails: render the placeholders as `helm.sh/hook: pre-install` resources (created once, never touched by upgrade, not deleted by uninstall; deleting the namespace is the clean-up). Two more rules: **no `checksum/config` annotation on the Deployment**, because the controller re-reads `controller.yaml` every 30 s without a restart (K§4.1) and a config-only `helm upgrade` should not roll the pod; and `helm uninstall` removes the credential Secret, so the operator **revokes the node on the primary** (`arb node revoke`), which the NOTES and runbook say (the credential is useless without a row, but a standing row with no cluster behind it is clutter and an open credential if the Secret was copied).

**Tests.** (1) `helm lint` + `helm template` for three value sets (default, tailscale, admission), piped through `kubeconform`; (2) a **parity test** (tag `:helm`, skipped when `helm` is absent, run in CI where it is installed) renders the chart and `InstallManifest.render/2` for the same inputs, normalises metadata that Helm adds (`app.kubernetes.io/managed-by`, `helm.sh/chart`) and compares the documents, the way `InstallManifestTest` pins its three golden files (`test/fixtures/k8s/install_{default,tailscale,admission}.yaml`); (3) the chart's ConfigMap is parsed back through `ControllerConfig` exactly as the manifests are (K§2.4 "Shipped"); (4) the `:k8s` e2e suite (`scripts/k8s-e2e.sh`, kind/k3d) installs the chart and runs the canary. The pod spec is **not** in the chart (the controller builds it at runtime, K§8), so the duplicated surface is about a dozen controller-side objects, not the hardening.

**KH5 as built (bd-2u4lpp).** `charts/arbiter-node/` (hand-written, OD9): `templates/bootstrap/` (PriorityClass, ResourceQuota, LimitRange, the four NetworkPolicies, optional RuntimeClass and ValidatingAdmissionPolicy + binding) and `templates/node/` (ServiceAccounts, Role, RoleBinding, placeholder Secrets, the two ConfigMaps, Lease, Service, Deployment, and the join Secret only for `--set-file join.token`), `mode: all|bootstrap|node`, fixed names, no `Namespace`. The Role gives the Deployment `get` only, and the Deployment sets `ARB_K8S_SELF_UPGRADE=false`, `ARB_INSTALL_METHOD=helm`, `ARB_HELM_RELEASE` and `ARB_CHART_REF`; the controller does not read the last three yet (KH8). `values.schema.json` rejects unknown keys at every level and refuses a `direct` install without `networkPolicy.controllerEgress.primaryCIDRs`, a `/0` entry, a `proxy` install without `reach.proxy.{url,cidrs}` and an admission policy without `registries[]`. `controller.yaml` is emitted from the values and only holds keys the controller's closed schema accepts, so `workers.serviceImageAllowlist` (named in the table above) is **not** a value: `ControllerConfig` rejects that key today. `registries[].pullSecret` feeds the flat `image_pull_secrets` list (per-pod scoping is KH13). `quota.hard` and `limitRange` are explicit figures for `concurrency.max=2` at the default worker size, copied from the `install_default.yaml` golden; a different shape needs the primary's numbers (KH9). `scripts/helm-chart-check.sh` (CI job `helm-chart`) runs `helm lint --strict`, `helm template | kubeconform -strict` for seven value sets and checks that eight bad value sets are refused; `helm_chart_test.exs` asserts the file-level rules and, where `helm` is installed, that the render has the same kinds and names as `InstallManifest.render/2` and that `controller.yaml` parses through `ControllerConfig`. Verified with helm v3.17.3 and kubeconform v0.6.7 against schema set 1.31.0, **not** on a cluster: H2 (`helm upgrade` over controller-written Secrets/ConfigMap/Lease) and H5 are still KH0's. The full document diff against the raw renderer is KH6.

**KH6 as built (bd-19mn3u).** `helm_chart_parity_test.exs` (`:helm`, skipped where `helm` is absent) renders `charts/arbiter-node` and `InstallManifest.render/2` for five equivalent configurations (default, tailscale, proxy, admission + pull secret, node selector + extra API CIDR + another namespace) and for `mode=bootstrap` / `mode=node`, and diffs every document field for field. The allow-list is the whole contract: the three Helm labels, the `Namespace` (the chart never templates it), the controller container's `imagePullPolicy`, and the `ARB_INSTALL_METHOD` / `ARB_HELM_RELEASE` / `ARB_CHART_REF` env vars; `controller.yaml` is compared as `ControllerConfig` parses it, minus `placement`, which the chart spells out and the raw renderer leaves to the controller. Quota and LimitRange are compared at the default shape only, because the chart carries explicit figures (KH9). The same file round-trips values through `ControllerConfig` (every exposed key comes back out of the controller's own parser; unset timeouts stay the controller's). `test/k8s/chart_install_e2e_test.exs` (`:k8s`, `scripts/k8s-e2e.sh`) installs the chart on kind/k3d, swaps the controller for the `socat` stand-in and runs the canary and readiness checks. **Not run by the author**: the worker that wrote it had no kind, k3d, kubectl or container runtime, so the e2e compiles and is guarded but has not been seen to pass on a cluster; the parity and round-trip tests were run against helm v3.17.3.

**KH9 as built (bd-3w7dji).** `GET /nodes/join/values.yaml` (anonymous, no secret; `NodeController.values/2`) takes the same query as `k8s.yaml`, goes through the same `InstallManifest.parse/1` and answers the same `422`; `InstallManifest.values/2` maps the parsed spec onto the chart's keys and takes `quota.hard` and `limitRange` straight from the raw renderer's `quota/1` and `limit_range/1`, so a `helm install` and a `kubectl apply` bound a namespace with the same numbers whatever `max`, `cpu` and `memory` are (the chart's static defaults are only right for `max=2`). The namespace is not a value (`--namespace`), and neither is `self_upgrade` (the chart's Role cannot patch its Deployment). `ClusterInstall.plan/2` gains a `helm` block (`namespace_command`, `secret_command`, `values_url`, `install_command`, and `split.bootstrap` / `split.node` for the cluster-admin split: releases `<name>-bootstrap` and `<name>`), and the **`nodes.registry` gate is relaxed for Helm**: `plan` succeeds without it, `manifest_url` / `apply_command` / `image` are `nil` with `registry_set: false`, and `values.yaml` answers `503` only when an admission policy or a pull secret needs a registry prefix. The modal opens on the **Helm** tab (**Raw manifests** is the other, with a note when it is unavailable), warns instead of refusing without `nodes.registry`, and has the "my cluster admin is someone else" toggle. `arb node add --kind cluster --format manifests|helm|values` (default: manifests, Helm while `nodes.registry` is unset): `helm` prints the §20.6 steps (`-o` saves the values file and installs from it), `values` writes the file to stdout and needs `--token-file` or `-o` so the token never shares stdout with it. Not built: the `--set-file` automation variant, the per-registry pull-secret and tailscale-key `kubectl create secret` lines (the operator creates those Secrets by name), and a rendered-chart check of the values (no `helm` in the build environment: the values are checked against `values.schema.json`'s keys and the raw renderer's numbers, **not** run through `helm template`).

### 20.6 B. Cluster-admin or not, and what "Add node → Kubernetes" shows

**What needs cluster-admin.**

| Object | Scope | Who | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Namespace` + Pod Security labels | cluster | cluster-admin | creating namespaces and labelling them is cluster-level |
| `PriorityClass arbiter-worker` | cluster | cluster-admin | cluster-scoped; **shared by every install on the cluster**, so a second install must set `priorityClass.create=false` (Helm refuses to adopt an object another release owns) |
| `RuntimeClass` (optional) | cluster | cluster-admin | cluster-scoped, and needs `runsc` on the nodes first (§20.3) |
| `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy` + `Binding` (optional) | cluster | cluster-admin | cluster-scoped, Kubernetes ≥ 1.30; one per namespace (it matches on the controller's service-account name, which contains the namespace) |
| `ResourceQuota`, `LimitRange` | namespace | cluster-admin **by design** | Kubernetes' built-in `admin` role can *read* but not write quota ("does not allow write access to resource quota", the role's documented behaviour); `LimitRange` is, I believe, likewise read-only for `admin` **[H3]**. They are guardrails: if the principal who runs the controller could edit its own quota, the quota would bound nothing |
| `NetworkPolicy` ×4 | namespace | a namespace admin *could*; grouped with the guardrails | a namespace admin can delete them, which the canary then reports as `netpol_unenforced` (fail closed) |
| ServiceAccounts, `Role`, `RoleBinding`, Secrets, ConfigMaps, `Lease`, `Service`, `Deployment` | namespace | namespace `admin` | a namespace admin holds every permission the controller's Role grants, so RBAC escalation prevention lets them create the binding **[H3]** |

That is `install_manifest.ex:268-285`'s split, unchanged: bootstrap = guardrails, node = the controller. `mode: bootstrap` and `mode: node` are two Helm releases (`<name>-bootstrap`, `<name>`) run by two principals; `mode: all` is one release by a cluster-admin and is the default because on mesaana the operator is cluster-admin and one command beats two. The UI offers a "my cluster admin is someone else" toggle that prints the two commands.

**Namespace ownership: the chart does not template the `Namespace`.** Helm stores its release record *in* the release namespace, so the namespace must exist first; `--create-namespace` makes it without labels, and a chart that also templates it fails ("exists and cannot be imported into the current release: invalid ownership metadata"; known Helm behaviour [H5]). So the namespace and its PSA labels are the first command of the install, a cluster-admin act, and the guarantee is not "the chart labels it" but **"the node is `degraded: psa_unenforced` until it is"** (§20.2, KH4). OD5 lists the alternatives (a bootstrap release that lives in another namespace; making the label a hook).

**The exact commands the modal shows** (tab **Helm**, the default; the tab **Raw manifests** keeps today's two commands from `cluster_install.ex:107-129`). Name `mesaana`, namespace `arbiter-workers`, release `0.2.31`, chart reference from a new setting `nodes.chart_ref` (default derived from the stamped `ARB_BUILD_RELEASE_REPO`, e.g. `oci://ghcr.io/<owner>/charts/arbiter-node`):

```sh
# 1. cluster-admin, once: the namespace and its Pod Security labels
kubectl create namespace arbiter-workers
kubectl label namespace arbiter-workers \
  pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce=restricted pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce-version=latest \
  pod-security.kubernetes.io/audit=restricted pod-security.kubernetes.io/warn=restricted

# 2. the join token: read from your terminal, never in argv, history or Helm's release record
read -rs T && printf %s "$T" | kubectl -n arbiter-workers create secret generic arbiter-join --from-file=token=/dev/stdin

# 3. install (the values file carries no secret; the numbers are computed by the primary)
curl -fsSL "https://arbiter.example.ts.net/nodes/join/values.yaml?name=mesaana&max=2" |
  helm install mesaana oci://ghcr.io/<owner>/charts/arbiter-node --version 0.2.31 \
    --namespace arbiter-workers -f -
```

Step 2 before step 3 is deliberate (the Secret exists when the pod first starts), but the order is **not load-bearing**: the Deployment mounts the Secret `optional`, kubelet refreshes secret volumes, and the controller is required to **re-read the token file while it has no credential** (KH10; today it reads once at start, **[H6]** to check). The token's 15-minute TTL starts when the modal mints it, so the modal shows the countdown it already has. When the operator also needs a pull secret per registry (§20.10) or the tailscale key, the modal appends their `kubectl create secret` lines, each reading the secret from the terminal, between steps 2 and 3. A `--set-file` variant sits under "automation" with the §20.5 warning. The modal's gate on `nodes.registry` (`node_controller.ex:97-98`, `cluster_install.ex:51`) is replaced by a *warning* "no worker registry configured: this node can run no job until a repo has one" (§20.10), because a chart install no longer needs the registry for its own image.

### 20.7 B. Who owns the controller's image after install

**The conflict.** K9 gives the controller `get`/`patch` on its own Deployment and has it set its `controller` container's image when the primary says `outdated` (`self_upgrade.ex:54-81`). A chart install makes the Deployment a Helm-managed object whose image is `image.tag` in the release. Both writers cannot own one field:

* After a self-upgrade the live image is `X'` and the release record says `X`. `helm upgrade` computes its patch from old manifest, new manifest and live state: if the new chart's `image.tag` is unchanged from `X`, Helm sees no change to that field and **leaves `X'` in place**, silently; if it is `X''`, Helm sets `X''`, discarding the self-upgrade. Neither is wrong, both are surprising, and `helm get manifest` / `helm diff` now lie about what runs.
* `helm rollback` restores the manifest, which patches the image back to `X`... unless the field did not change in Helm's view, in which case nothing happens. A rollback that rolls back nothing is the worst failure a rollback can have.
* `SelfUpgrade` also refuses an image from another repository (`self_upgrade.ex:92-93`). A chart install pulling `ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-controller` while the primary names `<nodes.registry>/controller:<v>` (`cluster_install.ex:79`) would be refused anyway.

**Options.**

| | How | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| **(1) Helm owns; self-upgrade off (recommended)** | The chart sets `ARB_K8S_SELF_UPGRADE=false` and renders the Role without `patch`. The node reports `caps.install = "helm"` (+ `release`, `chart`); the primary shows **`upgrade available: helm upgrade <release> <chart_ref> --version <v> -n <ns> --reset-then-reuse-values`** instead of `kubectl set image`. | the operator runs a command per node per release (or runs Flux/Argo with a semver range, which works because chart version == release, still one owner). The node is `outdated` until then; the skew rules (§6) keep new work off an incompatible one. |
| (2) Controller owns | the chart deploys once; `helm upgrade` is documented as "don't". | Helm cannot be told to ignore one field; either `lookup` the live image into the template (empty under `helm template`, `--dry-run` and every GitOps renderer, so it breaks exactly where it is wanted) or accept drift. Rejected. |
| (3) Both, with detection | the controller notices Helm's annotation and defers to it sometimes. | complexity for an ambiguity; there is no "sometimes" a rollback can rely on. Rejected. |
| (4) The primary drives `helm upgrade` | | a cluster credential on the primary, i.e. exactly the standing secret K§12 spends so much effort to avoid. Rejected. |
| (5) GitOps | Flux `HelmRelease` / Argo `Application` tracking `~0.2` | not an alternative to (1) but its automation: Helm (via the GitOps engine) is still the single owner. Documented, supported, not built by us. |

**Why this adds no risk and removes a little:** the chart's Role has no `patch` on the Deployment, so a compromised controller cannot repoint its own image (K§2.4's "no added blast radius" argument becomes "less blast radius"). **Detection** is explicit rather than inferred: the chart sets `ARB_INSTALL_METHOD=helm`, `ARB_HELM_RELEASE={{ .Release.Name }}` and `ARB_CHART_REF` on the Deployment; the controller reports them in `hello` (`caps.install`), `Overview.upgrade_fields/2` (`overview.ex:195-220`) branches on them and a helm install **never** gets `kubectl set image` (that command on a Helm-owned Deployment is the bug above). The raw-manifest install keeps K9 exactly as shipped. Moving a raw install to Helm is not designed here: re-installing is the supported route (revoke the node, delete the namespace, run the Helm commands with a fresh token); an adoption guide (`meta.helm.sh/release-name` annotations) is a runbook item if the operator wants it. **OD2.**

### 20.8 C. The controller image and the chart: published per release

**Controller image to GHCR.** The release workflow (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, which today ends at `softprops/action-gh-release`, `:206`) gains a job after `build`, with `packages: write` (and `id-token: write` for signing):

1. Build `ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-controller:<version>` from the **same tarball it just published** and the **same Containerfile** the primary uses, `Pipeline.controller_containerfile/0` (`worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:297-307`: debian trixie-slim + `ca-certificates libstdc++6 libncurses6 openssl`, `ADD release.tar.gz`, uid 10001, `ENTRYPOINT ["/opt/arbiter/bin/arbiter","start"]`). One source of truth: a mix task prints that file, so CI and the primary cannot drift; `FROM` is pinned by digest at build time (`Image.pin/2` does that on the primary, CI resolves the digest with the registry client and records it in an image label).
2. `helm package charts/arbiter-node --version <v> --app-version <v>` and `helm push … oci://ghcr.io/<owner>/charts`.
3. Sign both with cosign keyless (GitHub OIDC), and let the chart accept `image.digest`. Optional (KH7), not a gate.

What is in the image: the open-core release tarball only (Apache-2.0, `docs/licensing-model.md`): no `claude`, no deps seed, no provider secret. That is what makes a **public** GHCR package acceptable and what the K§11 rejection of "CI-published GHCR images" (private-repo pull credentials in every cluster, no image for a `--local` deploy) did not weigh. The release is built for x86_64 only (one ubi8 job), so the image is `linux/amd64`; an arm64 node needs the arm64 release job that §18 lists as "not filed" **[H4]**.

**Three notes.** (a) GHCR packages created by a workflow are not necessarily public even when the repository is; the first release publishes, the operator checks the package page, and flips visibility once if needed **[H7]**; the pull-secret path works either way. (b) The primary-pushed `<nodes.registry>/controller` (`publisher.ex:125`, a boot task) **stays** for the raw-manifest fallback, a `--local` deploy and an operator who mirrors images into a private registry (`image.repository` overrides the chart default). (c) A chart install no longer needs `nodes.registry` to exist, so `ClusterInstall.server_opts/1` (`cluster_install.ex:49-57`) and the 503 at `node_controller.ex:97-98` stop gating the *Helm* tab on it; the *Raw manifests* tab still does.

**The worker base on GHCR (used by §20.9's `prebuilt`).** The shared base (`Image.base_containerfile/1`, `worker/image.ex:107-128`: Debian trixie, `tini`, `socat`, uid 10001, the Erlang `arb` needs, `PodScripts.image_install()`) holds no secret and no provider CLI. The release job publishes it as `ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-worker-base:<base-hash>` where `<base-hash>` is the primary's own tag hash (`image.ex:248-262`), so a repo's CI can `FROM` the exact base a primary would have built. KH14.

### 20.9 C. Worker images: per repo, never a catch-all

**The problem in the code.** All worker images, for every repo, go to one repository, `<nodes.registry>/worker` (`pipeline.ex:90,138,175`), under one credential (`registry.ex:36-57`), are pulled by every pod with one flat pull-secret list (`pod_config.ex:72`, `pod_spec.ex:597`), and `PodSpec.image/2` accepts only refs under `cfg.registry <> "/"` (`pod_spec.ex:285-289`). The seed layer (`DepsCache` output copied to `/opt/arbiter/seed`, `pipeline.ex:161-186`) holds a repo's **private dependency sources**, and the CLI layer holds the real `claude` binary. So the thing that must be private (private deps, the CLI) is the thing pushed to a shared repository with one shared read credential. The operator's direction is that this be per repo.

**Config.** A new `image` block under `worker.repos.<repo>` (`tasks/workspace/config_schema.ex:410`, deep-merged over a workspace-level `worker.image`, per-repo wins):

```yaml
worker:
  repos:
    vstim:
      image:
        source: prebuilt                       # auto | containerfile | generated | prebuilt   (default: auto)
        ref: registry.gitlab.com/vstim/app/arbiter-worker:main    # prebuilt only: a tag (moving) or @sha256
        registry: registry.gitlab.com/vstim/app/arbiter           # where Arbiter pushes this repo's images
        credentials_ref: secret:GITLAB_REGISTRY                    # workspace secret: push (and ref-resolve) credential
        seed: primary                          # primary | image | none   (see below)
    arbiter:
      image: {source: containerfile, registry: ghcr.io/ryan/arbiter-worker, credentials_ref: secret:GHCR_PUSH}
```

`auto` is today's behaviour exactly: `containerfile` when the default branch has `.arbiter/Containerfile`, else `generated` (`image.ex:264-270`). So **adding the block is optional and nothing changes without it**, except the repository name (below). Validation reuses `Settings.valid_registry?/1` (the rule behind `nodes.registry`, `settings/registry.ex:277-283`); `ref` is required for `prebuilt` and refused otherwise; `credentials_ref` follows the existing convention (`"secret:<key>"`, `config_schema.ex:122,533-535`) and must name an existing workspace secret (`arb workspace secret set`, `docs/git-credentials.md:30-31`) whose value is `username:password`, so no credential ever sits in the config JSON.

| `source` | The definition | Built by | Lives in | Supply-chain rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `containerfile` | `.arbiter/Containerfile` on the **default branch's committed tree** | the primary (`Image.Builder`), empty build context | `<registry>/<repo>/worker` | unchanged: `git cat-file blob <default>:.arbiter/Containerfile`, never `HEAD` or a worktree, `FROM`s digest-pinned (`image.ex:20-30`) |
| `generated` | `.tool-versions` (`erlang`, `elixir`, `nodejs`) on the default branch, else the configured default toolchain | the primary | `<registry>/<repo>/worker` | unchanged |
| `prebuilt` | `ref`, an image **the repo's own CI builds** | the repo's CI; the primary only overlays | the CI's registry (toolchain) + `<registry>/<repo>/worker` (overlay) | `ref` lives in operator-owned workspace config, not in the repo, so a worker cannot move it; the content is whatever CI pushed, so (a) a tag is resolved to a digest at plan time and every pod pins the digest, (b) the image's `org.opencontainers.image.revision` must be an **ancestor of the default branch tip** in the primary's clone, else `image_unavailable{prebuilt_not_on_default}`: CI cannot push a feature-branch build under the tag the primary trusts |

**What the repository is called.** `<registry>/<repo-slug>/worker` (`<repo-slug>` as the private `slug/1` in `image.ex:279`, which becomes public), for every source and whether the registry is per repo or the install-wide fallback. Two repos can never share a repository; two repos with the same name in different workspaces on one registry are refused at config time by a doctor row (a shared repository would be the catch-all again). Moving from `<registry>/worker` is a one-time re-push; refs already handed to running pods are digest-pinned and unaffected.

**`prebuilt` needs a contract**, because a worker image is not just a toolchain: the pod runtime (K§8.2, K7) runs `seed`, `snapshotter`, `tini`, `socat` with OpenSSL, as uid 10001 with a passwd entry and `/home/arbiter`, with `claude` and `arb` in `/opt/arbiter/cli`. The repo's CI satisfies it by building **`FROM ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-worker-base:<hash>`** (§20.8); the image carries the label `arbiter.runtime-contract=1` and the primary checks it (and `skopeo inspect` for the revision label) before it uses the image. A contract the primary cannot verify is `image_unavailable{contract_mismatch}` with the missing item named, and the doctor has an `image` row per repo that does this check without a run. The primary then **overlays** the CLI layer (`pipeline.ex:114-144`) and, depending on `seed`, the seed layer, and pushes the result to `<registry>/<repo>/worker`; the toolchain layers are not pulled by the cluster from the CI registry at all, since the overlay is pushed with them.

**The deps seed layer, per mode** (`seed:`):

* `primary` (default, and the only mode for `containerfile`/`generated`): `DepsCache.ensure/4` builds `deps/` and `_build/` inside the image on the primary and the seed layer is `COPY`d over it (`pipeline.ex:161-186`, K§10.4). Private dependency credentials (Hex organisation keys, deploy tokens) are on the primary, where they already are. *(Corrected by §21.3.2, bd-7wlsq3: the seed job is handed no credential, so a repo whose dependencies need one is not seeded by the primary; and a `prebuilt` image's own `ENV` does reach the job.)*
* `image`: the repo's CI ran `mix deps.get && mix deps.compile` with **its** credentials and put the result at `/opt/arbiter/seed` in the image; the primary skips the seed layer. The image labels `arbiter.seed-lock=<sha256 of mix.lock>`; a mismatch with the default branch's lock at plan time falls back to `primary` and says so (a stale seed is a slow run, not a wrong one: Mix recompiles). This is the mode that moves private-dep access entirely out of the primary.
* `none`: no seed; cold compile. For repos without a lockfile, or when the image is the artefact and the tree compiles in seconds.

`seed_paths` beyond `deps`/`_build` keep K26's rule (excluded and listed by the doctor, `pipeline.ex:221-250`).

**KH14 as built (bd-3ojmcv).** `Arbiter.Worker.Image.Prebuilt` (`worker/image/prebuilt.ex`) is `Image.plan/3` for `source: prebuilt` (`Image.plan/3` delegates to it; the plan has `source: :prebuilt`, `base: nil` and `prebuilt: %{ref, digest, revision, seed, seed_note}`). It trusts an image in three steps, any failure being `image_unavailable{…}` with the reason named:

1. *Tag → digest.* `skopeo inspect --no-tags docker://<ref>` (`podman pull` + `image inspect` without skopeo), the repo's registry login passed as a 0600 auth file; the answer (digest and labels) is cached for five minutes. The plan's `tag` is `<ref-without-tag>@sha256:…` and its hash covers the digest and the seed mode, so a moved tag is a new image and nothing already pinned moves. A `ref` already pinned `@sha256:` must come back as that digest.
2. *Contract.* The label `arbiter.runtime-contract=1` (set by `Image.base_containerfile/1`, so anything built `FROM ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-worker-base:<hash>` inherits it) and a full 40/64-hex `org.opencontainers.image.revision`; otherwise `{:contract_mismatch, "<the missing item>"}`.
3. *Ancestry.* `git merge-base --is-ancestor <revision> refs/remotes/origin/<default>` (else `refs/heads/<default>`) in the primary's clone, re-run on every plan (only the registry answer is cached); a revision that is not an ancestor, or that the clone does not know, is `{:prebuilt_not_on_default, %{revision, branch}}`.

`seed: image` compares the label `arbiter.seed-lock` with the sha256 of the default branch's `mix.lock` and falls back to `primary` (plan note `prebuilt.seed_note`, shown by the doctor) on a mismatch or a missing label; `seed: none` ships no seed layer; `seed: primary` (default) runs `DepsCache` over the pulled image. `Pipeline.publish_image/3` for a prebuilt plan pulls the pinned CI image (auth file, never argv), builds the CLI layer and the seed layer on it and pushes both to `<registry>/<repo>/worker`; there is no toolchain layer and `Builder` is not involved. A prebuilt repo needs a registry node (`caps.image = "registry"`): `ContainerSpawn.preflight_image/3` answers `{:image_unavailable, {:prebuilt_needs_registry, repo}}` for any other node. Local (podman) runs still use `Image.ensure/3` and ignore `image.source`, as since KH11. The doctor gets an `image <repo>` row per prebuilt repo (`Prebuilt.report/1`, in `GET /api/nodes` → `registry.images`): ref, digest, revision, seed mode and note, or the refusal. The CI registry login is the repo's `credentials_ref`, so a `ref` on a host other than the push `registry` is read anonymously.

*What a repo's CI does* (the contract, for `.gitlab-ci.yml` / GitHub Actions):

```dockerfile
FROM ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-worker-base:<hash>        # <hash> is the tag the release job publishes (`mix arbiter.containerfile worker-base --pin --hash-out`); it carries arbiter.runtime-contract=1
# the toolchain the repo needs (Erlang/Elixir/Node)
ARG GIT_SHA
LABEL org.opencontainers.image.revision=$GIT_SHA        # full commit id of the default-branch commit CI built from
# optional, for `seed: image`: mix deps.get && mix deps.compile into /opt/arbiter/seed, then
LABEL arbiter.seed-lock=<sha256sum mix.lock>
```

Push it from the default branch only (a feature-branch job that pushes the trusted tag is refused by the ancestry check, but keep the tag clean anyway), then set `worker.repos.<repo>.image` `{source: prebuilt, ref: <registry>/<path>:<tag>, registry: <where Arbiter pushes>, credentials_ref: secret:…}`. Not verified here: a real `skopeo`/`podman` round trip against a registry and a pod pulling the overlay on a cluster (the tests stub both); `arb doctor` on the running primary is the first real check.

### 20.10 C. Registries and pull secrets, per repo

**Resolution order** for where a repo's images go and which credential pushes them: `worker.repos.<repo>.image.registry` → `worker.image.registry` (workspace) → the install-wide `nodes.registry` settings, which stay as the fallback so an existing install changes nothing (KH11/KH12). `Registry.fetch/1` (`registry.ex:36-57`) grows a `repo`/`workspace` context; its auth file is already keyed by registry host (`registry.ex:122-123`), so a run that must read the CI registry and write the repo registry gets one file with two `auths` entries, still 0600, never on argv. The doctor's `nodes.registry` row (reachability, last publish) becomes a row per registry in use.

**Private means private.** The repository holds private dependency sources and the real `claude` binary (K§11: "belongs only in the operator's **private** registry"). Today that is a help-text sentence (`settings/registry.ex:150-155`). Each registry in use gets an **anonymous-read probe** (an unauthenticated `GET /v2/<repo>/tags/list` or a manifest `HEAD`, the same reachability call `Publisher.status/1` makes, `publisher.ex:199-225`): a repository that answers anonymously is a `[warn]` naming it, and refused for `seed != none` or any layer containing the CLI unless the operator sets an explicit `public_ok: true` on that registry. GHCR packages default to private; GitLab follows the project's visibility; the probe is the only reliable check across vendors.

**In the cluster** the unit is the registry **prefix**, not the install:

* The chart value `registries: [{prefix: registry.gitlab.com/vstim/, pullSecret: gitlab-vstim}, {prefix: ghcr.io/ryan/, pullSecret: ghcr-arbiter}]` renders into the controller ConfigMap (`ControllerConfig` gains `registries`; the single required `registry` key, `pod_config.ex:37`, becomes derived for compatibility).
* `PodSpec.build/2` sets `imagePullSecrets` to the **one** secret whose prefix is the longest match for *that pod's* image ref, replacing the flat list every pod gets today (`pod_spec.ex:597`). A pod for repo B never references repo A's pull secret; with Pod Security and the admission policy forbidding Secret volumes (K§7) the secret stays unreachable to the pod either way, but a reference is also a request to the kubelet to use that credential, and least privilege is cheap here. No match is `refuse{image_unavailable, "no pull secret for <registry> on this node"}`, which the primary already maps to a hold or local fallback (`publisher.ex:148-149`).
* `PodSpec.image/2` (`pod_spec.ex:285-289`) accepts a digest-pinned ref under **any** configured prefix. The admission policy's image rule (`admission_policy.ex:59-66`, today `<registry>/`, `docker.io/library/`, extras) is generated from the same list, so the controller and the API server agree on what may run.
* The node reports its prefixes in `hello` (`caps.registries`). `Placement` treats a node without the repo's registry prefix as not a candidate for that repo's runs (like `netpol_unenforced`), so a repo whose images live in the vstim GitLab registry never reaches a node that cannot pull from it; `arb node show` and the nodes page list them.
* **Pull credentials are read-only and separate from push credentials.** The primary holds the push token (Cloak, workspace secret); the cluster holds only a read-only deploy token the operator creates (`read_registry` on GitLab; for GHCR a token with `read:packages` **[H13]**). The modal prints, per registry, a create command that keeps the password off argv (`kubectl create secret docker-registry … --docker-password=` would put it there):

```sh
read -r U && read -rs P && printf '{"auths":{"registry.gitlab.com":{"username":"%s","password":"%s"}}}' "$U" "$P" |
  kubectl -n arbiter-workers create secret generic gitlab-vstim --type=kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson --from-file=.dockerconfigjson=/dev/stdin
```

**The two targets named in the direction:** `vstim` → its private GitLab registry (the cluster already has the `gitlab-registry` pull secret per §13, so `prefix: registry.gitlab.com/vstim/` maps to it with no new secret on mesaana); `arbiter` → GHCR (`ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-worker/…`, private package, a read token in a second Secret). One cluster, two prefixes, two secrets, and each pod references exactly one.

**KH13 as built (bd-9jspj1).** `PodConfig` gains `registries: [%{prefix, pull_secret}]` (prefix ends in `/`, unique; `pull_secret` optional = anonymous pull) and `ControllerConfig` accepts it (`registries:` in `controller.yaml`); `registry` stays the primary-supplied default prefix. `PodConfig.image_prefixes/1` is the allowed list (the `registries` prefixes plus `registry/`) and `PodConfig.pull_secrets/2` the one secret of the longest matching prefix, which `PodSpec` writes as the pod's only `imagePullSecrets` (a ref under `registry` and under no `registries` prefix keeps the legacy flat `image_pull_secrets`). `PodSpec.image/2` accepts a digest-pinned ref under any of those prefixes and service images may come from them too; a pinned ref under none is `{:bad_spec, {:registry_not_configured, ref}}`, which the controller refuses as `refuse{image_unavailable, "no pull secret for <host> on this node"}`. `AdmissionPolicy.manifests/1` takes `registries:` (prefixes) and `InstallManifest` passes the install's list, so the API server and the pod builder agree. The controller reports its prefixes as `caps.registries` in `hello` (the config's prefixes and `registry/`); `Placement` requests carry `:registry` (`Placement.image_registry/2`: the repo's, else the workspace's, else `nodes.registry`) and `Placement.registry_covered?/2` drops a cluster node whose `caps.registries` does not cover it. A node that reports no `caps.registries` (an older controller) and every machine node are not filtered. `arb node show` and the node page list the prefixes. The install side: `registries=prefix=secret,…` on `GET /nodes/join/k8s.yaml` / `arb node add --registries` / the Add-node form, and the plan carries one `pull_secret_commands` entry per registry with a secret (the `read -r U && read -rs P && printf … | kubectl create secret generic … --from-file=.dockerconfigjson=/dev/stdin` form above, so the password is never on argv). The Helm chart renders `registries[]` into the controller's `registries` list (it no longer flattens them into `image_pull_secrets`). Not verified here: the chart template (no `helm` in the worker sandbox; CI's `helm-chart` job and `helm_chart_test.exs` cover it) and a real two-registry pull on a cluster (KH0/H13).

### 20.11 C. Where builds happen, and where the seed layer fits

| | Toolchain layer | CLI layer | Seed layer | Credentials it needs | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A. The primary** (today) | `podman build` of the pinned Containerfile, empty context | `podman build` over it | `DepsCache` → `COPY` | registry push (primary); private-dep keys (already on the primary, but never handed to the seed job: §21.3.2) | **default for `containerfile` / `generated`** |
| **B. The repo's CI** (`prebuilt`) | the repo's pipeline, on its runners, cached there | the primary overlays (small, changes with each `claude` update) | `seed: image` (CI) or `primary` | CI's push credential (CI-side); the primary needs *read* to resolve and pull `ref`, and *write* for the overlay | **default for `prebuilt`** |
| **C. In-cluster BuildKit** | a `buildkitd` Deployment | | | a push credential in the cluster, deps credentials in the cluster, egress to Hex/registries from the build pod | **rejected for v1** |

**Why not in-cluster BuildKit** (the ticket asks for the weighing): (1) *rootless* BuildKit needs `seccomp`/AppArmor unconfined (or `--oci-worker-no-process-sandbox`) and mount capabilities inside a user namespace; Pod Security `restricted` forbids `Unconfined` and added capabilities, so it cannot run in the workers' namespace and would need its own, weaker one **on a cluster that hosts vstim prod**: the opposite of what this section is for. (2) It puts a **push credential and private-dependency credentials in the cluster** (K§12's principle is that nothing but the node credential, the CA key and an optional tailscale key lives there). (3) A build is a CPU spike next to prod, outside the quota that bounds workers. (4) The seed layer needs `mix deps.get` egress, which the deny-all policy removes (K§10.4's rejection of the in-cluster seed Job still applies). (5) The primary already has podman, `DepsCache` and the credentials. Revisit only if the primary cannot build (a laptop primary with no spare CPU); then run BuildKit in a **separate namespace or cluster**, never in the workers' namespace. The alternative that removes the primary's overlay work without a cluster builder is *CLI as a separate init-copied image* (`/opt/arbiter/cli` populated from a small shared image into an `emptyDir`, so `prebuilt` images are used verbatim and nothing is pushed per repo): it adds a ~200 MB copy and an init container to every pod and a second private image every cluster must pull; recorded, not recommended for v1. **OD4.** §21.2.4 weighs it again, with the image-volume variant (OD14), for machine nodes as well, and keeps the layers.

**Resource and failure shape.** A→ `Publisher.ensure_ready/2` stays single-flight with its 10-minute bounded wait and `fallback/2` (`publisher.ex:70-80,148-149`); B adds one registry round trip (resolve tag → digest, cached for a few minutes) and a pull of the CI image to the primary on first use of each digest (843 MB class, once). Either way a pod only ever pulls a digest-pinned ref the primary has already confirmed exists.

### 20.12 Unverified assumptions (H-series; K1–K26 and U1–U20 still apply)

Nothing in §20 was run: no cluster was contacted and no tool was installed. KH0 and KH1 are the spikes that settle these, on a **disposable** cluster (the operator's k3s only with an explicit go-ahead, as K§0 ruling 1 already says).

| ID | Assumption | If it fails | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | `topologySpreadConstraints` with `nodeTaintsPolicy: Honor` / `nodeAffinityPolicy: Honor` is accepted on the target Kubernetes and a soft constraint spreads two pods over two unequal nodes | drop the two policy fields (tainted nodes then count as domains: acceptable with `ScheduleAnyway`) | KH0 |
| H2 | Helm's three-way merge preserves controller-written `data` on the placeholder Secrets/ConfigMap and the Lease's holder across `helm upgrade` | render the placeholders as `pre-install` hooks (§20.5) | KH0 |
| H3 | Built-in `admin` cannot write `ResourceQuota` (documented) **or `LimitRange`** (believed), and can create the controller's `RoleBinding` (it holds every permission the Role grants) | move `LimitRange` into the namespace part of the split, or document the extra grant | KH0 (`kubectl auth can-i … --as`) |
| H4 | No target node is arm64 | an arm64 release job (§18, "not filed") before those nodes can join | operator (`kubectl get nodes -o wide`) |
| H5 | A templated `Namespace` clashes with `--create-namespace` / pre-existing namespaces under Helm 3 | if Helm adopts cleanly, the chart can own the namespace and OD5 becomes simpler | KH0 |
| H6 | Kubelet refreshes an `optional` Secret volume that appears after the pod started, and the controller re-reads `/etc/arb/join/token` while it has no credential (today it reads once at start; not checked in this ticket) | KH10 makes it so; until then the Secret must exist before the pod starts (the step order in §20.6 already does this) | KH0, KH10 |
| H7 | A GHCR package published by Actions for a public repository is public; otherwise the operator flips it once | pull secret for the controller image | first release after KH7 |
| H8 | The k3s kubelet on mesaana has a `podPidsLimit` | document the exposure; the sandboxed runtime is the real answer | operator (`kubectl get --raw /api/v1/nodes/<n>/proxy/configz`) |
| H9 | `mix deps.compile && mix test` for arbiter and vstim under `runsc` costs a multiple of runc the operator can accept | stay on runc (OD1 option a); the section's recommendation already is | KH1 |
| H10 | `runsc` supports `hostUsers: false` on the operator's containerd/runsc versions | the pod would need either a user namespace or the sandbox, and the admission policy and `pod_security.ex` assumed the former | KH1: **refuted** (k3d k3s v1.35.5, containerd 2.2.3, gVisor release-20261005.0, RuntimeClass `gvisor` → handler `runsc`): runsc rejects `hostUsers: false`, every pod hung in `ContainerCreating`. The sandbox replaces the user namespace, so the builder omits `hostUsers` for a runsc-handled class and the admission policy and PSA check relax for that class only (bd-27o4ls); `hostUsers: false` stays everywhere else |
| H11 | The K8 guard mounts (`mv`/`rm` → `EBUSY`/`EROFS`), the K2 netpol enforcement and seed gate, native sidecars and the Postgres sidecar all behave under `runsc` as under runc | the tamper guarantees (bd-6t7u81) cannot be claimed under runsc | KH1 |
| H12 | The chart's floor of Kubernetes 1.30 is right (first release with `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy` GA); Helm ≥ 3.14 for `--reset-then-reuse-values`; `helm install -f -` reads stdin | raise the floor / print the plainer command | KH0 |
| H13 | A read-only pull credential exists for GHCR that does not reach other packages (GHCR has historically needed a classic PAT with `read:packages`, which reaches everything its owner can read) | use a machine user that owns only the arbiter packages | operator, at KH13 time |
| H14 | A `LimitRange` `Pod`-type `max` and `maxLimitRequestRatio` still admit the worker pod with its init containers and native sidecars (the effective pod request sums them in a way I have not recomputed) | set `max` on `Container` only | KH0 |
| H15 | An anonymous-read probe works against the GitLab and GHCR registries (they answer `/v2/` with a 401 and a token realm even for public repositories, so "anonymous" means fetching an unauthenticated bearer token first) | probe via the vendor API instead | KH12 |

### 20.13 Open decisions for the operator

Each has the options, what each costs, a recommendation, and which tickets wait on it. **Until the operator answers, the recommendation is what the tickets in §20.14 assume.**

**OD1. gVisor / `runtimeClassName`: default on, off, or required?**
* *(a) Off by default, supported, measured first (recommended).* Costs: mesaana's prod neighbours share the kernel until a class is turned on. Gains: installable on a runc-only k3s today, no scheduling restriction, no unmeasured slowdown on builds. Trigger to revisit: KH1's benchmark and guard-mount results (H9–H11). **KH1 result so far (H10, H11):** under runsc the guard mounts, the netpol canary, native sidecars and the Postgres sidecar all behaved as under runc, so isolation under runsc holds; the one blocker was `hostUsers: false` (refused by runsc), now fixed by omitting it for a runsc-handled class (bd-27o4ls). The readiness `runtime_class` row also stopped trusting the dry run: it creates a real short-lived pod under the class and reports a sandbox-creation failure (`FailedCreatePodSandBox`) by name. OD1 stays *off by default*.
* (b) On whenever a `RuntimeClass` named `gvisor` exists. Behaviour that changes when someone installs a class. Not recommended.
* (c) Required on shared clusters (install refuses / node `degraded: shared_kernel` without it). The right end state for prod-adjacent clusters **if** KH1 comes out well. Blocks the first install on mesaana until `runsc` is on every node.
