* Needed from the operator: do you want to install `runsc` on mesaana's nodes at all, and what slowdown on the arbiter and vstim test suites is acceptable? Blocks: KH4 (the row), KH1.

**OD2. Who owns the controller image after a chart install?**
* *(1) Helm; K9 self-upgrade off; the node says `helm upgrade …` (recommended; §20.7).* Costs a command per node per release unless Flux/Argo does it. Gains: `helm diff` / `rollback` are truthful; no `patch` verb on the controller.
* (2) The controller (Helm deploys once). Drift or `lookup` tricks that break under `helm template`/GitOps.
* (3) Both, with detection. An ambiguity with no safe tie-break.
* (4) The primary runs `helm upgrade`. A cluster credential on the primary.
* Raw-manifest installs keep K9 whichever is chosen. Blocks: KH5 (Role), KH8.

**OD3. Registry scoping: install-wide, per workspace, or per repo, and where the credentials live?**
* (a) Install-wide `nodes.registry` only (today). One repository, one credential, one pull-secret list for every repo: the catch-all the ticket forbids.
* (b) Per workspace. Better, but repos of one workspace on different registries (vstim → GitLab, a sibling → GHCR) cannot be expressed.
* *(c) Per repo, falling back to the workspace and then to `nodes.registry` (recommended; §20.9–§20.10).* Push credentials as **workspace secrets** (existing mechanism, `docs/git-credentials.md`), pull credentials as operator-created per-registry Secrets in the cluster mapped by **prefix**, one secret per pod.
* (d) A new registry-credential resource instead of workspace secrets: a second place secrets live; reject unless workspace secrets prove too coarse.
* Blocks: KH11, KH12, KH13.

**OD4. Where do builds happen?**
* *(a) The primary for `containerfile`/`generated`; the repo's CI for `prebuilt` (the primary overlays CLI and seed) (recommended).* No new infrastructure; private-dependency credentials stay where they are.
* (b) CI for everything. Needs the CLI (a real provider binary) in the repo's CI and a CI-side seed for each repo; moves a provider binary into CI logs and caches.
* (c) In-cluster BuildKit. Rejected for v1 (§20.11): needs privileges the PSA-restricted namespace forbids, a push credential and private-dep credentials in the cluster, and CPU outside the quota, next to prod. Revisit only for a primary that cannot build, and then in a separate namespace or cluster.
* Blocks: KH14.

**OD5. The cluster-admin split, and who owns the namespace.**
* *Split* — (a) one chart, `mode: all | bootstrap | node`, default `all` (recommended): one command when the operator is cluster-admin, two releases when not. (b) Always one install, cluster-admin required. Simplest, but a namespace admin cannot be delegated the controller. (c) Two charts. A second artifact to version and publish for the same split `?part=` already expresses.
* *Namespace* — (a) the chart does not template it; the first command creates and labels it, and a missing label makes the node `degraded: psa_unenforced` (recommended). (b) A bootstrap release that lives in another namespace and templates the `Namespace` (Helm can own it; the release record lives elsewhere; an extra namespace to explain). (c) A pre-install hook. Hooks cannot label a namespace they did not create without cluster rights the hook's service account would then hold.
* Needed from the operator: on mesaana, are you cluster-admin and is `all` enough? Blocks: KH5, KH9.

**OD6. Controller image source.** *(a) GHCR, public, built by release CI; `image.repository` overridable for a mirror (recommended).* (b) Primary-pushed `<nodes.registry>/controller` only (today): needs the registry before the first install, and a self-hosted primary to have run. (c) Both, GHCR the default. This is (a) plus the primary-pushed image kept as the raw-manifest path, which is what §20.8 does. Blocks: KH7. Operator action: set the GHCR package public once if H7 says so.

**OD7. Spread strictness and memory burst.** Spread: *`ScheduleAnyway` (recommended)* vs `DoNotSchedule` (never schedules a pod on a full node when a roomier one exists, but wedges to `Pending` on unequal nodes like mesaana's 8 CPU and 4 CPU pair). Memory: keep `limits.memory` = 2× `requests.memory` (today, with the new `LimitRange` ceiling; recommended) vs limit = request (strict, no burst overcommit on a node that runs prod; would need a larger request, so a smaller `max_concurrent` on small nodes). Blocks: KH2.

**OD8. Worker egress.** *None beyond the controller (recommended; §20.4).* The alternatives are CIDR allowlists for model APIs and git hosts (brittle: CDN-fronted, and a direct path around the primary's policy and audit) and FQDN policies (Cilium/Calico only, still a direct path). The operator's own sentence, "egress allowlist (model APIs, git hosts, registry, primary only)", is satisfied by: model APIs and git hosts by the primary's `Egress` policy through the bridge, the registry by the node's kubelet (outside NetworkPolicy), the primary by the controller's narrowed egress. Blocks: KH3.

**OD9. One source of truth for the install.** *(a) A hand-written chart plus the raw-manifest renderer, with a CI parity test (recommended).* (b) Generate the chart from Elixir: Ymlr cannot emit Go-template conditionals, so the "generator" is a hand-written chart in disguise. (c) Drop the raw manifests: the ticket keeps them. Blocks: KH6.

**OD10. How far to trust a `prebuilt` image.** *(a) Resolve the tag to a digest at plan time; require the revision label to be an ancestor of the default branch tip; verify the runtime-contract label (recommended).* (b) The operator pins `ref: …@sha256:…` by hand and moves it with `arb`. Strongest, and most work for the operator; the ancestry check still applies and is the part that catches a pinned-but-unmerged build. (c) Trust the tag. A feature-branch CI job that pushes the same tag would run unreviewed code in the worker image with the CLI overlaid. Rejected. Blocks: KH14.

| # | Title | Scope | Diff. | depends_on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KH0 | **Spike A: Helm, RBAC and spread semantics on a disposable cluster** | settle H1, H2, H3, H5, H6, H12, H14 with scripts and raw outputs in `docs/design/k8s-spike/` (the K1 layout): soft spread over unequal nodes, `helm upgrade` over controller-written Secrets/ConfigMap/Lease, `admin` vs quota/limitrange/rolebinding, namespace + `--create-namespace`, optional Secret appearing late, `--reset-then-reuse-values`, `-f -`, `LimitRange` with native sidecars. Per-row GO / NO-GO / GO-WITH-FALLBACK; amends §20.12 | D3 | none |
| KH1 | **Spike B: gVisor on a disposable node** | install `runsc` on a throwaway k3s/kind node; run K8 guard mounts, K2 netpol and seed gate, K10 sidecars, `hostUsers: false` under a `RuntimeClass`; benchmark `mix deps.compile && mix test` for arbiter and vstim, runc vs runsc, wall time and CPU. Settles H9–H11; the numbers go to the operator for OD1 | D3 | none. **Run (k3d k3s v1.35.5, containerd 2.2.3, gVisor release-20261005.0):** H10 refuted and fixed (bd-27o4ls, OD1); H11 holds (guard mounts, netpol canary, native sidecars, Postgres sidecar isolated under runsc) |
| KH2 | **Spread and hard per-pod bounds** | `placement.spread` in `PodConfig` and `PodSpec` (`topologySpreadConstraints`, `nodeTaintsPolicy`); `LimitRange` `max` / `maxLimitRequestRatio` (Container and Pod), quota `limits.ephemeral-storage`, worker `limits.ephemeral-storage` (`install_manifest.ex`, `pod_config.ex`, `pod_spec.ex`); update goldens, the PSA checker and the K4 conformance tests; canary uses it too | D2 | KH0 (H1, H14) |
| KH3 | **Network allowlist: controller egress, `reach: proxy`, metadata probe** | replace `0.0.0.0/0:443` with `primaryCIDRs` for `reach: direct`; `reach: proxy` (sets `ARB_NODE_PROXY`, egress to the proxy only); pin the tailscale sidecar image; canary probes `169.254.169.254`; manifests and goldens | D2 | none |
| KH4 | **Readiness: `psa_unenforced`, runtime-class row, shared-kernel line** | `psa` failure degrades the node (`readiness_monitor.ex`, same audited override as `allow_unenforced_network`); `hello` reports `runtime_class` and whether it exists; doctor prints the shared-kernel line; docs | D2 | none (KH1 informs the wording) |
| KH5 | **The `arbiter-node` chart** | `charts/arbiter-node/`: bootstrap and node templates mirroring `InstallManifest` (fixed names, `mode`, no Namespace, no `patch` on the Deployment, `ARB_INSTALL_METHOD`/`ARB_HELM_RELEASE`/`ARB_CHART_REF`), `values.yaml` + `values.schema.json` (unknown keys rejected), `NOTES.txt`, `kubeVersion`, the join/`--set-file` handling; `helm lint` + `kubeconform` in CI | D3 | KH0, KH2, KH3 |

## 21. One self-sufficient published worker image for every remote target (bd-7wlsq3)

> Status: **proposed**, written 2026-10-25 on `main` at `a37ac19e1a`. Operator direction (2026-10-25): every container shipped to a remote target must be completely self-sufficient, so that a worker runs on any node or in any pod without placement having to know what a host has installed. Docs only: no code, config, node or registry was touched. Code citations are `file:line` on that commit, with paths relative to `apps/arbiter/lib/arbiter/` unless they start with `apps/` or `docs/`. What I relied on but did not observe is **[S#]**, indexed in §21.10. Section numbers `§21.N` are local to this section. It amends §1 (decision 1), §2 (first row), §7.3, §10.4.4, §11, §13, K§11, K§12, §20.9 and §20.11, each of which now points here. The implementation tickets are §21.9 (`SI0`–`SI14`, plus `SI4a` and `SI9a`), filed by the coordinator from the PR description. The open decisions continue §20.13's numbering (OD11–OD14, §21.8); until the operator answers, the recommendation is what the tickets assume.
>
> **Today's machine-node path, observed from inside.** The primary placed the run that wrote this section on `ryan-oryx-pro`, so its own container shows the mechanism this section replaces. `/proc/self/mountinfo` binds `/home/ryan/.arbiter-node/files/b73676430af1/claude` at `/opt/arbiter/cli/claude` (Claude Code 2.1.310, 291,638,640 bytes), `files/800b7a6739cb/arb.escript` (2,581,850 bytes) and `files/57cfb43fa14e/arb` beside it, and `/run/arbiter/secrets.env` from the node's tmpfs (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/arbiter-node/<run>/`, a 3.1 GiB tmpfs). The checkout's `.git/arbiter-deps-cache` names the node's own image `localhost/arbiter-dev/beam-1.20.4-29.1.1:a5d425f5e914` and a seed job the node ran itself (`src=/home/ryan/.arbiter-node/deps-seed/…`), which left `deps` at 52 MB and `_build` at 145 MB. The image's `/opt/arbiter` holds `bin` (`seed`, `snapshotter`), `otp` (120 MB) and `cli` (the base's empty directory, where the three binds land), and no `seed`.

### 21.0 Decisions in one screen

| # | Decision | Losing option(s) | Open? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The published per-repo run image is the one artifact for every remote target.** A machine node pulls `<registry>/<repo>/worker@sha256:…` exactly as a cluster pod does (in the end `caps.image = "registry"` on every node, with a dual path while it migrates, §21.6), and stops building from a plan and fetching CLI files. A node then needs rootless podman, git and a route to the primary and the registry, and nothing installed for any provider. | Keep build-from-plan plus the file shelf for machines (a node keeps needing distro, Docker Hub and Hex egress to build and seed, and two nodes can hold different bits under one tag); a second, machine-only image format. | — |
| 2 | **Runs on the primary keep the fast path** (local `Image.ensure/3` build, CLI bind-mounted read-only), made to agree with the image by construction: the same base and toolchain tag, the same `/opt/arbiter/cli` layout, one CLI-set resolver for the mounts and the layer, and the set recorded on every run. | Converge local runs on the published image: a CLI update would wait for a layer build before the next local run, a local-only install would build every repo's full run image it never needed, and no isolation is gained (a read-only bind and a read-only layer are the same boundary). | **OD11** |
| 3 | **The CLI layer carries the CLI set: `claude`, `codex` (the vendored ELF with `rg`, `bwrap` and its sibling executables) and `arb` (launcher and escript).** One `COPY` layer per CLI, ordered by churn and **above** the seed layer (toolchain → seed → codex → claude → arb), built deterministically; each CLI's version and sha256 are image labels. A later provider joins by one rule (§21.2.3). | The CLI under the seed layer (today: a CLI update rebuilds and re-pushes the seed image per repo); one layer for all CLIs (an `arb` update would re-push the 292 MB `claude` per repo); a shared CLI image (OD14). | OD13, OD14 |
| 4 | **A run is pinned to one CLI set for its whole life.** The digest `prepare_remote/1` picks rides every re-open of that worker, and the run records the digest and the CLI versions. A CLI update reaches the next run, never a running one. Nodes never resolve tags: they learn a digest from `assign`, and ahead of time from a new `image.prefetch` the primary sends after it publishes. The operator can hold the install on a CLI set. | Moving tags on nodes; re-resolving the CLI per open. | — |
| 5 | **Injected per run, never baked:** provider credentials and the Codex login, the short-lived git token a run may carry (a GitHub App or GitLab project token), the MCP bearer and worker token, `worker_env`, the generated config, the prompt, the clone, a resumed session's transcript and (machine nodes) the registry pull credential. No layer holds a credential; the only private content (the `claude` binary, dependency sources) lives in the repo's own private repository (§21.3). | Baking config or a token into a per-repo layer "because the repository is private anyway" (a pull credential would then be a provider credential). | — |
| 6 | **Codex rotation on a node or in a pod: the primary keeps a mirror of the run's `auth.json`.** The live copy is on tmpfs (node) or a memory `emptyDir` (pod); the agent or the snapshotter uploads it whenever its bytes change and once more before the run's directory goes, under a per-run **credential grant** that outlives the run's stream (cancel, owner death, quiesce); the primary stages it and runs the unchanged `AuthSync.sync/2` against the real login; re-opens `sync` and `pull` against the mirror; `AuthSync.Reaper` tracks it. | The node holding the real login (a provider secret at rest on a node); a copy with no carry-back (the first refresh strands the operator's login); access-token-only delivery (unverified CLI behaviour, S5). | **OD12** |
| 7 | **A machine node gets a pull credential per pull, never at rest:** read-only, and reaching no repository that holds runs the node may not take (a repo-level credential always qualifies; a workspace- or install-wide one only for a node allowed to run everything it reads), delivered with `assign` (or `image.prefetch`), written as a 0600 `--authfile` on the run's tmpfs and deleted after the pull. The push credential never leaves the primary. | A standing per-node pull secret (a second secret at rest on every node, one per registry); the primary relaying layers (0.5–1 GB per toolchain per cold node through the primary, §7.3's rejected `save`/`load` in another form, plus a registry to implement). | — |
| 8 | **Cold and offline nodes:** a pull is bounded by a pull-sized prepare timeout, keeps going after a refusal, and is warmed by `image.prefetch`; a node holding the digest runs without the registry; placement prefers a warm node among equals. **Image GC on a node removes only what the agent pulled** (a ledger), never an image a live, retained or gate run still needs, and keeps the newest two per repository. | GC by name pattern or `podman image prune` (the node's owner may have images of their own, and a node can be enrolled to two installs); no GC (today node-built images, the file cache and the deps cache only grow). | — |
| 9 | **Placement:** `:non_claude_provider` becomes a set of providers with a remote credential path (Claude now; Codex once §21.3.3 lands, per node by `caps.credential_sync`). What disappears is the dependency on the primary's installed CLI and on the node's build environment; agy stays local for its credential, not its binary (§21.5). The replacements are checks: the preflight refuses an image whose labels do not list the run's provider, the node checks the pulled image's runtime contract and CLI set, and the doctor gets image-source and registry rows. | A node-side "provider installed" probe (it would recreate the per-host coupling this removes). | — |
| 10 | **Rollout:** CLI set and layer order → machine-node pull with a dual path → deps from the seed layer, GC, prefetch → Codex on machine nodes → Codex in pods → retire build-from-plan, the file shelf and `/nodes/files`. | A flag day (every node and the publish path at once, with no fallback while it beds in). | — |

The base leaves `/opt/arbiter/cli` empty and on `PATH`. | `worker/container_spawn.ex:552-563`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:582-655`, `worker/arb_cli.ex:123`, `worker/arb_cli.ex:154`, `worker/image.ex:117-137` |

Cluster runs get the same `cli` mounts, which the pod builder ignores. | `worker/container_spawn.ex:911`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1143-1152`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1415-1416`, `nodes/files.ex:19`, `node_agent/files.ex:17-21`, `node_agent/run.ex:745`, `node_agent/run.ex:932` |

Builds pass no `--timestamp`. The toolchain image is pushed into each repo's own repository. | `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:111`, `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:147-167`, `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:179-187`, `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:237`, `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:389`, `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:105` |

Podman itself is wired for Claude and Codex only. | `nodes/placement.ex:227-230`, `nodes/placement.ex:285`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1004-1005`, `worker/sandbox.ex:82-85` |
| Codex, locally | A per-run `CODEX_HOME` holding a copy of the source login; `AuthSync.sync/2` at every re-open (then `pull/2`), at teardown, and from `AuthSync.Reaper` when the owner dies; one install-wide source home. | `agents/codex/config_dir.ex:205-226`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:777`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:799`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1630`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1784-1786`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1799-1803`, `agents/codex/auth_sync.ex:94`, `agents/codex/auth_sync.ex:106`, `agents/codex/auth_sync/reaper.ex:32-40` |

| A run's end on a node | `finish/2` stops the container, cleans up (secrets removed), uploads the final checkout and only then reports `exit`; a quiesced run is retained on the node and not uploaded, "nothing on the primary would accept it". The offline pre-push gate rebuilds a container later from the run's remembered mounts (`NodeAgent.Exec`). | `node_agent/run.ex:1033-1043`, `node_agent/exec.ex:38` |
| Upload windows | The node's uploads for a run are accepted while the run's stream and checkout context live, or while a recovery is pulling it; a run whose owning Worker dies is cancelled and dropped at once. | `apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_checkout_controller.ex:268`, `nodes/session.ex:539`, `nodes/session.ex:949`, `nodes/session.ex:758` |
| Remote teardown | `ContainerSpawn.teardown/1` matches a local container run only; a remote run falls through to `:ok`. | `worker/container_spawn.ex:1799-1807` |

A node's own seed job keys on the **checkout's** `mix.lock` at `HEAD`, the published layer on the default branch's. | `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:242`, `worker/deps_cache.ex:313`, `worker/deps_cache.ex:335`, `apps/arbiter/priv/k8s_pod/seed:231`, `worker/deps_cache.ex:464` |
| Node GC | None for images, the file cache or the deps cache: the agent starts `Image.Builder` and nothing that prunes; `Image.prune/1` and the weekly refresh run on the primary. | `node_agent/supervisor.ex:121`, `worker/image.ex:678` |
| Prepare bound | 25 minutes, sized for a cold toolchain build on a node. | `nodes/session.ex:96` |
| Harness version | Unknown up front for a container or remote spawn (the host binary is not the one that ran), so only Claude's in-stream `init` reports one. | `worker/claude_session.ex:339-340`, `agents/harness_version.ex:16` |
| `/nodes/files` | Serves the agent tarball by hash (`Agent.find_by_sha/2`) and the CLI shelf. Nothing in the tree fetches the tarball by hash: the join script and the upgrader use `/nodes/agent/<version>.tar.gz`. | `apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_controller.ex:380-381`, `apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_controller.ex:401`, `apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/router.ex:101`, `nodes/agent.ex:80`, `nodes/join_script.sh.eex:538`, `node_agent/upgrade.ex:133` |
| Node egress | "A node needs outbound access to image registries and distro mirrors", because it builds and seeds. | `docs/remote-workers-runbook.md:64` |

**What actually decides where a provider runs today.** The framing "the provider must be installed on the node" is right in effect but not in mechanism, and the difference decides what disappears. (1) No placement rule looks at a node's software. (2) A remote run's CLI comes from the **primary's** host, shelved and fetched (`remote_cli/2`), so installing a CLI on a node changes nothing. (3) Codex never reaches a node because `:non_claude_provider` and `remote_provider/1` refuse it before any CLI is looked at, and because nothing would carry its rotated login back; installing it on oryx would not have changed that. (4) What does depend on the node is its **build environment**: it builds the base (`apt-get` from Debian mirrors), the toolchain (Docker Hub) and the deps seed (Hex) itself, so a node without that egress can run nothing, and two nodes can hold different bits under one tag. (2) to (4) are what this section removes.

### 21.2 The artifact, the CLI layer and how a CLI update moves

#### 21.2.1 One image for every remote target

| Layer | Content | Keyed by | Visibility |
|---|---|---|---|
| base | `Image.base_containerfile/1`: Debian trixie, build tools, `git`, `socat`, `tini`, `tmux`, uid/gid 10001 `arbiter` with `/home/arbiter`, `/opt/arbiter/otp` (arb's Erlang), `/opt/arbiter/bin/{seed,snapshotter}`, an empty `/opt/arbiter/cli` on `PATH`, `arbiter.runtime-contract=1` | its content hash | public as `ghcr.io/<owner>/arbiter-worker-base` (§20.8): no CLI, no secret |
| toolchain | the default branch's `.arbiter/Containerfile` or `.tool-versions`, empty build context | `<hash12>` | in each repo's own repository |
| seed | `DepsCache` output at `/opt/arbiter/seed/{deps,_build,mix_home}` | `seed<lock12>-<sp8>` | per repo, private |
| CLI | `/opt/arbiter/cli/*`, the CLI set of §21.2.3, one layer per CLI | `cli<set8>` | per repo, private |

Tags: `<hash12>` (toolchain), `<hash12>-seed<lock12>-<sp8>` (seed) and the run image `<hash12>-seed<lock12>-<sp8>-cli<set8>` (`<hash12>-cli<set8>` when no seed shipped), which is what `assign.image.ref` pins by digest. The repository is `<registry>/<repo-slug>/worker` (KH11) on the registry `Registry.resolve/3` picks (KH12). For `prebuilt` the CI image replaces the first two layers (KH14), unchanged.

**A machine node uses it as a pod does.** A new agent lists the sources it can take, `caps.image_sources: ["registry", "build"]`, and the primary chooses per run: the registry when the node lists it, the repo has a registry with a pull credential this node may be given (§21.4) and `nodes.machine_image_source` is `auto`; else the plan, exactly as today (§21.6). That replaces `registry_node?/1`'s single read of `caps.image` (`worker/container_spawn.ex:1133`); once the plan path is retired, machine agents advertise `caps.image = "registry"` as clusters do. For a registry run, `registry_image/4` already sends a pinned `ref` and no plan (`worker/container_spawn.ex:1121-1133`), so the primary side mostly *removes* work: `remote_cli/2` returns no `cli` mounts, and `prebuilt_node/3` stops refusing machine nodes. The agent's `default_image/2` (`node_agent/run.ex:727-743`) gains a pull clause: `podman image exists <ref>` (a digest reference, so presence is exact), else `podman pull --authfile <tmpfs file> <ref>` (§21.4); then one `podman image inspect` of its labels, refusing `image_unavailable{contract_mismatch, <item>}` unless `arbiter.runtime-contract` is a version the agent supports, `arbiter.cli-set` equals the spec's new `image.cli_set`, and the run's provider has its `arbiter.cli.<name>.*` labels. The container then runs the digest reference with `--pull=never`, as every run does today (`worker/container.ex:173`). The plan clause and the agent's `Image.Builder` stay until §21.6 step 6.

**Deps on a machine node come from the image's seed layer, with a fallback.** Today the agent runs a networked seed job keyed on the checkout's own lockfile (`node_agent/run.ex:661-698`, on the node's own tag at `node_agent/run.ex:675`; `DepsCache.seed_checkout/3` at `HEAD`, `worker/deps_cache.ex:464`). The seed layer is built from the default branch's lockfile, and three things make it a drop-in:

1. **The layer carries the stamp.** `DepsCache.install/3` refuses a directory without `.complete` (`worker/deps_cache.ex:313`) and relocates the copied `_build` manifests and `priv` links from the stamp's `src=` line (`worker/deps_cache.ex:335`); without that, Mix rebuilds every dependency and the offline pre-push gate fails on the NIF ones. Today's layer copies the artifact directories only (`worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:242`), so SI5 adds `/opt/arbiter/seed/.complete` (lock hash, toolchain tag, `src`). The pod `seed` script has the same gap (it copies the layer with `cp -a` and relocates nothing, `apps/arbiter/priv/k8s_pod/seed:231`); SI5 closes it there too.
2. **One extraction per seed layer, not per run image.** The agent extracts `/opt/arbiter/seed` once per seed layer (`<hash12>-seed<lock12>-<sp8>`, the CLI suffix excluded, so a CLI update re-extracts nothing) into `<node_home>/deps-cache/` (a one-shot hardened container with that directory mounted, or `podman create` plus `podman cp`), then `DepsCache.install/3` reflinks and relocates it into each run's shadow, as now.
3. **A lockfile the branch changed falls back to today's path.** When the checkout's `mix.lock` does not hash to the stamp's lock, the agent runs today's seed job for the checkout's own lock, which needs Hex egress from the node; a node without that egress leaves the run to fetch and compile the changed dependencies through the run's egress proxy, and the offline gate then finds what the run compiled. No lockfile, or a seed the publisher skipped, leaves the run to compile, as today.

**What a node needs afterwards:** rootless podman with the memory controller delegated, `git`, the tailnet route to the primary, and HTTPS to the registries its workspaces use (plus the test-service images' registries, or a mirror of them). No distro mirrors, no Docker Hub for builds, no provider CLI, no provider login, and no Hex except for a branch that changed its lockfile (above). `docs/remote-workers-runbook.md:64` changes with it (SI14).

#### 21.2.2 Runs on the primary: keep the fast path (OD11)

| | Keep the fast path (recommended) | Converge on the published image |
|---|---|---|
| CLI update | the next spawn binds the new binary | the next local run waits for a CLI-layer build, or runs the previous set |
| What a local-only install builds | the toolchain image it already builds | every repo's seed and CLI layers too, which it never needed |
| Deps | `DepsCache` reflinked into the worktree, near free on btrfs | the same cache, or a copy out of the layer (a few hundred MB per run; no reflink out of an overlay) |
| Fidelity with remote | same base, toolchain tag, layout and resolver; the bound CLI can be one publish ahead of the newest published set | bit-identical |
| Isolation | read-only bind | read-only layer: the same boundary |

Recommended: keep it. What converges is *what* is mounted, not *how*: one resolver (`Worker.CliSet`, new) answers both `cli_mounts/2` and the layer, the local mount binds the whole set as the layer carries it, and every run records the set it ran (hash and versions), whether from a bind or a layer. An opt-in for bit-for-bit parity while debugging a remote-only failure (`worker.image.local: published`, OD11 b) is a later ticket, not in v1.

#### 21.2.3 What the CLI layer holds, and the rule for the next provider

| In `/opt/arbiter/cli` | Source on the primary (v1) | Labels |
|---|---|---|
| `codex`, `rg`, `bwrap`, the `codex-*` siblings | the npm package's vendored ELF and helpers for the primary's arch, or a cargo/brew ELF as it is (`codex_mounts/1`, `native_codex/1`: `worker/container_spawn.ex:608-655`) | `arbiter.cli.codex.version`, `.sha256` (of `codex`), `.files` |
| `claude` | the host `claude`, links resolved (`worker/container_spawn.ex:582`) | `arbiter.cli.claude.version`, `.sha256` |
| `arb`, `arb.escript` | `ArbCli`'s launcher and the host escript (`worker/container_spawn.ex:563`) | `arbiter.cli.arb.version`; `arbiter.arb-otp` is already on the base |

Plus `arbiter.cli-set=<set8>` (the sha256 over the sorted `(name, sha256)` pairs, today's rule at `worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:147-162`) and `arbiter.cli-providers=claude,codex`. Each row is one `COPY` from a snapshot directory, in that order. A primary with no Codex publishes a set without it; its images cannot take a Codex run (§21.5), which is the right failure, and the doctor shows it.

**Why the primary's own binaries (v1) and not upstream releases (OD13).** They are what local runs use and what Arbiter's adapters run against on that host; they need no download-and-verify path per vendor; and "Codex is installed on the primary" is a one-host requirement every local Codex run already has. The per-host coupling the operator objects to is the *node's*, and that goes either way. The cost is that the published set follows the primary's auto-updater, so the publisher **snapshots** each set it publishes into `<image root>/cli-sets/<set8>/` with a manifest (name, version, sha256, source path), builds the layer from the snapshot, and keeps the newest five. An operator **hold** (`worker.cli_set.hold: <set8>`, an installation setting) makes the publisher build from that snapshot whatever the host has since installed: the rollback for a bad CLI release. OD13 (b), pinned upstream versions fetched and verified by the primary, would free even the primary from installing Codex; it is a follow-up.

**Shipped (SI1, bd-1hoo7j; OD13 b).** `Arbiter.Worker.CliSet` resolves the set from SI1a's cache of pinned upstream releases (`CliFetcher.Cache`), never from a CLI installed on the primary, and materializes it to `<image root>/cli-sets/<set8>/<cli>/` with a `manifest.json`. The local bind mounts (`ContainerSpawn.cli_mounts/2`), the remote file shelf (`remote_cli/2`) and the image layers (`Pipeline.cli_set/1`) all read it. The set hash is over file content (cli, file, sha256, mode), so a version bump with identical bytes moves nothing; the publisher's cache key is that hash. The image is toolchain, seed, then one `COPY` per CLI (`codex`, `claude`, `arb`) built with `--timestamp 0`; tags `<hash12>-seed<lock12>-<sp8>` and `<seed tag>-cli<set8>` (`<hash12>-cli<set8>` with no seed). Tags of the old graph are never rewritten or removed, so a digest already handed to a pod keeps resolving. Labels: `arbiter.cli-set`, `arbiter.cli-providers` and `arbiter.cli.<name>.{version,integrity,sha256,source}`. Not in SI1: the snapshot keep-five, the hold and the 10-minute watcher (SI2), and `ArbCli.runtime_otp/1` still reads the host `arb` for the base image's Erlang.

**Shipped (SI2, bd-a42gf8).** `worker.cli_set.hold: <set8>` makes `CliSet.resolve/1` read the set back from `<image root>/cli-sets/<set8>/manifest.json` (what the publisher, the mounts and the shelf all use), whatever the pins say now; `Bump` is suspended while it is set. A hold with no snapshot behind it falls back to the (frozen) pins. `Publisher.ensure_ready/2` records each successful publish in `Image.PublishLedger` (`<image root>/publish-ledger.term`), and `CliSet.Watcher` (at boot, every 10 minutes, and on the `worker.cli.versions` change a bump announces) republishes every repo in it from the last 14 days when the set hash moved. Still not shipped: the keep-newest-five snapshot pruning.

**The rule for a later provider** (qwen, grok, agy: each is a change to the set, never a new mechanism). A provider joins the set when all four hold:

1. podman has a wrap point for it (`Sandbox.module/2`, `worker/sandbox.ex:82-85`): a sandbox decision made per provider, separately;
2. its CLI is a self-contained executable for the image's arch, or it brings its own runtime under `/opt/arbiter/cli/<provider>.d/` (a Node-based CLI may not use the repo toolchain's Node, which a repo may lack or pin to something else); names do not collide in `/opt/arbiter/cli`, and a helper that would goes under `<provider>.d/`;
3. its credential can be injected per run and either does not rotate or has a carry-back like §21.3.3's;
4. its egress hosts are known (`@egress_infra`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:166`).

Qwen today: (1) has no wrap point, (2) is an npm package (it needs a vendored Node or a single-file build), (3) is an API key in the environment, which is easy. agy: (2) is unknown (agy-podman-backend.md §3, A1) and (3) fails (§21.5).

#### 21.2.4 How a CLI update propagates, and pinning

1. **Detect.** `CliSet` resolves at boot (a deploy restarts the primary) and every 10 minutes, and keys the set on content (sha256 per file) rather than on path, size and mtime (`worker/image/publisher.ex:150-155`).
2. **Republish eagerly.** A new set (with no hold in force) republishes every repo that published in the last 14 days, single flight per repo as today, instead of leaving the first dispatch to pay for it in `Dispatch.image_gate/6` (`worker/dispatch.ex:2871`).
3. **Push only what changed.** With the CLI layers on top in churn order and built with a fixed `--timestamp`, identical files give identical layer blobs **[S2]**: a Claude update rebuilds the `claude` and `arb` `COPY` layers per repo and pushes one new ~292 MB blob (the `arb` blob is unchanged and already in the registry, the `codex` layer below is untouched); an `arb` update pushes ~2.6 MB. The toolchain and seed images are reused by tag. Because the blob is the same in every repo, a registry that mounts blobs across repositories and a node that reuses layers by content transfer it once, not once per repo **[S2]**.
4. **Nodes learn a digest only from the primary.** `assign.image.ref` is always a digest the primary chose; a node never resolves a tag. After a publish yields a new run-image ref, the primary sends **`image.prefetch{ref, cli_set, pull_auth}`** (new, `caps.image_prefetch`) to each online, ready machine node that could take that repo's runs (workspace pin, labels, not draining). The agent pulls in the background, one pull at a time, and lists the digests it holds in its heartbeat (`hb.images`, newest 32). A cluster pulls on its first pod (`IfNotPresent`); a cluster pre-pull (a DaemonSet or a puller pod) is not designed here.
5. **A run uses one known set.** `prepare_remote/1` stores the digest in the request, and every re-open re-places the run from that request (`worker/claude_session.ex:2468-2470`), so the CLI never changes under a running worker; a resume and a fresh dispatch take the current set. The run row records `image_ref` and `cli_set` (hash plus versions; new columns). For a provider whose stream does not report its version (Codex), `harness_version` comes from the image label instead of staying unknown (`worker/claude_session.ex:339-340` skips container and remote spawns today), so G18's trust clock sees a Codex upgrade on a node.

**Rejected or deferred for the layer.** One layer for every CLI: an `arb` update would re-push `claude` per repo. The CLI under the seed: every CLI update rebuilds the seed image. **OD14, a shared CLI image** mounted as an image volume (`podman run --mount type=image,…`; a Kubernetes `image` volume): one push per CLI update whatever the repo count, and `prebuilt` images used verbatim, but two digests to pin per run, a second private repository every node and cluster must pull from, and an image-volume feature the target clusters are not known to have **[S3]**. The init-copied variant (§20.11, OD4's alternative) adds a copy of the whole set (about 300 MB with today's `claude`, not §20.11's 200) and an init container to every pod. Recommended: the layer now; revisit OD14 (b) when image volumes are generally available on every cluster target.

### 21.3 Baked versus injected

#### 21.3.1 The list

| Item | Baked or injected | Machine node | Cluster pod |
|---|---|---|---|
| OS, toolchain, `tini`, `socat`, uid 10001, `/opt/arbiter/{bin,otp}` | **baked** (base, toolchain) | | |
| `deps`, `_build`, Mix home | **baked** (seed layer, per repo) | extracted once per seed layer, installed (reflink and relocation) per run | copied by `seed` from `/opt/arbiter/seed` (relocation added by SI5) |
| `claude`, `codex` and its helpers, `arb` | **baked** (CLI layers, per repo) | | |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` / `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ARB_TOKEN`, `ARBITER_MCP_TOKEN`, `worker_env` values | injected | spec `secrets` → a tmpfs file sourced by the wrapper (§7.1) | `/boot` → a memory `emptyDir` file, sourced and deleted (K§12) |
| Codex `auth.json`, and the rest of the run's `CODEX_HOME` seed (`config.toml`, `AGENTS.md`, `rules/arbiter.rules`) | injected | spec `secret_files` (new) → the run's `CODEX_HOME` on tmpfs (rollouts in `sessions/` on disk); `auth.json` carried back (§21.3.3). `config.toml` rides here too because a provider table can carry a header token | `/boot` → `CODEX_HOME` on a small memory `emptyDir` (`sessions/` on `work`); carried back by the snapshotter |
| Git credential | injected only as a short-lived token minted per run and revoked when the worker exits: a GitHub App installation token for a pass that pushes from its container (`ARB_GIT_TOKEN`), or a GitLab project token, which every run kind of such a repo holds (`ARB_GIT_TOKEN` and `GITLAB_TOKEN`, `worker/git_credential.ex:1158`); a deploy key or a static token never leaves the primary (§11, `:scoped_git_credential`) | as `secrets` | as `secrets` |
| Claude's generated `settings.json` and `CLAUDE.md` | injected: they differ per workspace | `config_dir.files` | `/boot` seed files |
| `.mcp.json` (bearer replaced by `${ARBITER_MCP_TOKEN}`), skills | injected | `worktree.files` | `/boot` seed files |
| The clone | injected | seed bundle → shadow clone (§9) | seed bundle → `emptyDir` (K§10.1) |
| The prompt | injected | `prompt` mounts or the argv | the same, through `/boot` |
| A resumed session's transcript, redacted | injected | `GET /nodes/runs/:run/session` | — |
| Registry pull credential | never enters a container | spec `pull_auth` → a 0600 auth file on tmpfs, deleted after the pull (§21.4) | the operator's per-prefix `imagePullSecret` (KH13) |
| Node credential, CA key, tailscale key | never in a run | | |

#### 21.3.2 No credential in any layer, no private content in a shared or public one

The rule: a layer holds only public upstream content, the repo's own dependency sources and build artefacts, and provider CLI binaries, and the last two only in the repo's own private repository. A credential is never in a layer, private repository or not: a pull credential must never double as a provider credential. Layer by layer:

* **Base** (public on GHCR): generated text (`worker/image.ex:117-137`), every `FROM` a digest-pinned public image, no build arg and no context. Debian packages, the Erlang/OTP install arb runs on, Arbiter's own pod scripts. Nothing private.
* **Toolchain**: the default branch's committed Containerfile, or a generated one, built with an **empty context**, so it cannot `COPY` anything from the primary; the only build arg Arbiter passes is the base tag (`worker/image.ex:229`). A repo owner can still commit a secret into their own Containerfile: that is the repo's own content, in its own private repository, and nothing Arbiter injects.
* **Seed** (per repo, private), built from `DepsCache` output. The seed job "sees neither the repo, nor a credential" (`worker/deps_cache.ex:33`): its environment is `LANG`, `MIX_ENV`, `HEX_HOME` and `MIX_HOME` (`worker/deps_cache.ex:234-238`), its mounts a `git archive` export and a scratch home, and what is published is `deps/`, `_build/` (the repo's own apps pruned) and the scratch **`MIX_HOME`** (`.mix`, `worker/deps_cache.ex:199`), never `HEX_HOME` (`.hex`), where a `hex.config` holding an organisation key would live. So for a `containerfile` or `generated` image a primary-built seed holds public dependency sources and their compiled code only, and **a repo whose dependencies need a credential is not seeded by the primary at all**: its `deps.get` fails, the seed is skipped and the run compiles cold. (This corrects §20.9's "private dependency credentials … are on the primary": they may be, but the seed job is never handed them.) A `prebuilt` image is different: podman applies the image's own `ENV` (`Container.run/2` unsets nothing, `worker/container.ex:743`), so credentials baked into a CI image's environment reach the primary's seed job, and every run container, too.
  * Private dependencies therefore reach a layer only through a `prebuilt` image: its `seed: image`, or a credential in its environment. The contract (§20.9) gains these rules, checked by the primary for **every** `prebuilt` plan before it trusts the image (SI12): credentials only through build-secret mounts (`RUN --mount=type=secret`), never `ARG` or `ENV`, because an `ARG` used in a `RUN` is recorded in clear in the image history; the image config's `Env` and every history `created_by` are scanned for the values `WorkerEnv.secret_values/1` knows and for credential-shaped tokens (the rules `SessionHistory.seed_redacted/4` redacts by), and a hit refuses the image for every run (`image_unavailable{secret_in_image}`); the primary's seed job runs with the image's environment reduced to an allowlist **[S10]**; and `/opt/arbiter/seed` holds no `hex.config`, `.netrc`, `.git-credentials`, `id_*` or `*.pem`, and no `deps/*/.git/config` whose remote URL carries userinfo (a git dependency fetched with a tokenised URL keeps the token there). What remains, private dependency *sources*, is private content rather than a credential, and stays inside the repo's own repository (below).
* **CLI** (per repo, private): `COPY` from a snapshot directory holding only the binaries of §21.2.3. `claude` belongs only in the operator's private registry (K§11), so the CLI layers are private whatever the other CLIs' licences would allow.
* **Labels**: versions, hashes and provider names, nothing else.
* **Where the private layers live.** One repository per repo (`<registry>/<repo>/worker`, KH11), never shared between repos. The publisher refuses a configured registry that answers anonymous reads unless it sets `public_ok` (`worker/image/publisher.ex:107-129`), but that probe skips the install-wide `nodes.registry` (`origin: :install`, `worker/image/registry.ex:48`; the fall-through at `worker/image/publisher.ex:129`). SI12 extends it there, since machine nodes now pull CLI and seed layers from it too. Who reads a repository: the primary (its push credential), a cluster pod through that repository's prefix pull secret only (KH13), and a machine node for one pull with a read-only credential that reaches no repository whose runs it may not take (§21.4). The toolchain image is pushed into every repo's repository (`worker/image/publisher/pipeline.ex:105`): two repos may share a blob in a registry's storage, never access to a repository.
* **What a pull leaves on a node:** layers in the node's podman graph root (persistent storage), i.e. binaries and dependency sources, and no credential. Credentials stay on tmpfs (`node_agent/secrets.ex:110`) and leave with the run.

#### 21.3.3 Codex's `auth.json` on a node or in a pod (AuthSync at a distance)

The local problem (podman-worker-containers.md §4.2) with the run's copy now on another machine: the CLI refreshes its ChatGPT token in the copy and the server retires the refresh token the real file still holds.

**Three copies, one owner.** The *source* is the real login on the primary (`Codex.ConfigDir.source_home/0`, `agents/codex/config_dir.ex:226`). The *mirror* is the run's copy on the primary, `<run tmp>/codex-home/auth.json`, exactly where a local run keeps its copy: `prepare_remote/1` seeds it with `Codex.ConfigDir.seed_run_home/2` (`agents/codex/config_dir.ex:205-214`) as `run_dirs("codex", …)` does locally (`worker/container_spawn.ex:777`), and registers it with `AuthSync.Reaper` as `track_codex_auth/2` does (`worker/container_spawn.ex:799`). The *live copy* is on the node or in the pod, and it is the only one the CLI touches. Only a file on the primary ever meets the source (the mirror, or a staging file once the run's Worker is gone), so `AuthSync` itself does not change.

**Delivery.** The spec carries the mirror's bytes, with the rest of the run's generated `CODEX_HOME` files, as `secret_files` entries (new: `{path, content, mode}`, counted against the 64 KiB `secrets` cap, `node_agent/run_spec.ex:92`, and, like `secrets`, never logged). On a machine node a Codex run's `config_dir` mount says `"tmpfs": true` (new): the agent makes it a directory of its own on `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (refused unless that is a tmpfs, as `Secrets.write/3` is, `node_agent/secrets.ex:49-110`) instead of under `<node_home>/runs/<run>/` (`node_agent/run.ex:459`), so a refresh the CLI writes never reaches persistent storage, and binds a disk directory over `<CODEX_HOME>/sessions` so the rollouts do not sit in memory **[S11]** (the small files and `auth.json` fit easily in oryx's 3.1 GiB runtime tmpfs, **[S7]**). The directory holds a refresh token, so the agent removes it recursively, without following links, once the final upload is done; today's `Secrets.remove/2` deletes one file and then `rmdir`s (`node_agent/secrets.ex:81-82`), which a `CODEX_HOME` would survive. The offline pre-push gate, which `NodeAgent.Exec` rebuilds later from the run's remembered mounts (`node_agent/exec.ex:38`), gets an empty directory in its place: it never runs the CLI. In a pod, `CODEX_HOME` is a small memory `emptyDir` (`sizeLimit` 8 Mi: the small files and `auth.json`) mounted in `worker` and `snapshotter` and filled by `seed` from `/boot`, with `sessions/` a subpath of `work`.

**Carry-back (live → staging → source).** The agent (every 5 s while the container runs) or the snapshotter (on its 10-s command poll, `apps/arbiter/priv/k8s_pod/snapshotter:33`) compares the live file with the bytes it last delivered or sent, reading it only as a regular file in a real directory (the rule of `AuthSync.read_regular/1`, `agents/codex/auth_sync.ex:174`). On a change it `PUT`s them: a machine node to `PUT /nodes/runs/:run/credentials/codex` (new, node credential); a pod to `PUT :9444/credential` with the run's control leaf, which the controller relays byte for byte to the same route (K§10.3: the controller never parses a pod upload).

* **Authorized by a grant, not by the checkout context.** The checkout routes accept a run only while its stream and context live or while a recovery is pulling it (`apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_checkout_controller.ex:268`, `nodes/session.ex:539`, `nodes/session.ex:949`), and a run whose owner dies is dropped at once (`nodes/session.ex:758`): exactly when a last rotation must still land. So the node's `Nodes.Session` holds a **credential grant** per Codex run: made at assign, naming the node, the run and the source path (in memory, never read from a request), kept for 30 minutes past the run's exit, cancel, owner death or quiesce, re-made from the run row after a primary restart, and dropped once the run is collected.
* **Staged, then synced.** The primary caps the body at 64 KiB, writes it to a staging file it owns (an exclusive temp file `rename/2`d over the mirror while the Worker lives, a fresh one in a scratch directory after) and calls **`AuthSync.sync(source, staged)`** (`agents/codex/auth_sync.ex:94`). Its trust rules are unchanged and remain the boundary: adopted only while the source exists, for the same account, strictly newer and at most five minutes ahead of the primary's clock (`agents/codex/auth_sync.ex:65`, `agents/codex/auth_sync.ex:131`). Because the newest login wins, it does not matter whether a late upload lands before or after a reaper's sync.
* **Ordered on the node.** The final credential upload comes before the cleanup that removes the directory and before the `exit` report, as the final checkout already precedes `exit` (`node_agent/run.ex:1039-1043`), so a re-open's spec is never built from a mirror that lacks the last rotation. A quiesced run uploads it before it is retained (`node_agent/run.ex:1033`); bytes not yet accepted wait on tmpfs only, never in `Retained`.

Codex's rollouts (`sessions/**`, newly admitted by the extractor, `nodes/transcripts.ex:130`) ride the transcript upload as Claude's `projects/**` do; the credential never does, since the extractor takes only `*.jsonl`.

**Re-open (mirror → live).** A nudge or an auto-resume re-places the run from the stashed request (`worker/claude_session.ex:2468-2470`). Before `remote_spec/3` builds the new spec, the primary does what `wrap_port/1` does locally (`worker/container_spawn.ex:1630`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1784-1786`): `sync` (mirror → source), then `pull` (a strictly newer source, rotated by a sibling run, replaces the mirror); the spec then carries the mirror. A re-open is a fresh container, so the node's previous live copy is gone and the mirror is the continuity.

**End and death.** A teardown sync for a remote run is new work: `ContainerSpawn.teardown/1` matches local container runs only (`worker/container_spawn.ex:1799-1807`). `AuthSync.Reaper` syncs the mirror when the owner dies by any means (`agents/codex/auth_sync/reaper.ex:32-40`), and `RunTmp.Reaper` flushes it before it removes the run's tmp dir, as today, because the mirror lives in that dir; the node's final upload, which can arrive after both, still lands through the grant.

**Primary restart.** A run kept on the node keeps its live copy; the agent retries the upload with backoff while the route is down, keeping only the newest bytes, and the re-made grant takes them once the primary is back, Worker or not. Adoption is not extended here: today only a Claude implement run is adopted (§10.4.4), so a Codex run on a node is quiesced and collected across a restart, its credential uploaded first.

**What this cannot fix, stated.** (1) A rotation on a node that is lost (or rebooted: tmpfs), or in a pod evicted before its final upload (K§10.3: about 2 s on an eviction), strands the source: the next refresh anywhere fails "refresh token was already used" and the operator runs `codex login` on the primary. The window is one poll interval plus an upload **[S8]**. (2) Two runs holding the same token, where the first refresh retires it, is the residue the local design already accepts (podman-worker-containers.md §4.2). (3) A compromised node holds a refresh token, i.e. access to the account until the operator revokes it: §11's "bounds persistence, not exposure" for Codex. The mitigation §11 gives Claude, a dedicated account for remote runs, needs a per-account Codex source home (there is one install-wide source, `agents/codex/config_dir.ex:226`): OD12 (b). Access-token-only delivery (strip the refresh token, so a run can rotate nothing) would close (1) and (3), but whether the CLI then finishes a long run or dies at its first 401 is unverified **[S5]**: OD12 (c), a spike.

A login that does not rotate (an API key in `auth.json`) needs none of this: the live copy never changes, so nothing is uploaded, and `sync` would answer `:unchanged` if it were. Claude needs none of it either: its credential is `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in the environment, which a run does not rotate, and its config dir is seeded with no credential file (§11 unchanged).

### 21.4 Registry access for machine nodes

**Pull credential: per pull, read-only, never at rest.**

* **Config.** A read credential next to each push credential: `worker.repos.<repo>.image.pull_credentials_ref` and `worker.image.pull_credentials_ref` (a workspace secret holding `username:password`, like `credentials_ref`), and for the install-wide fallback `nodes.registry_pull_username` and `nodes.registry_pull_password` (Cloak-encrypted, write-only, operator-only, like `nodes.registry_password`). It should be a registry-native read token scoped to the repo's repository (a GitLab deploy token with `read_registry`; for GHCR, H13's caveat applies).
* **Reach, not just read-only.** One credential on the install-wide registry reads every repo's `<registry>/<repo>/worker`, seed and CLI layers included, so handing it to a node pinned to one workspace would let that node read another workspace's private dependencies. The rule: a node is given a pull credential only if every repository it can read holds runs that node may take. A repo-level credential always qualifies; a workspace-level one goes to a node whose pin admits that workspace; the install-wide pair only to an unpinned node (which may already be handed runs of every workspace). Otherwise the run takes the plan path during the migration and is refused afterwards (`no_pull_credential`, below).
* **The push credential never goes to a node**, even when no pull credential is configured: a node holding it could overwrite the run images every other node and the cluster then run. No pull credential means `image_unavailable{no_pull_credential}` at the preflight, which `Publisher.fallback/2` turns into local or a hold (`worker/image/publisher.ex:199`), plus a doctor row; until the plan path is retired, such a repo is served by the plan instead (§21.6 step 2).
* **Delivery.** `assign` and `image.prefetch` carry `pull_auth: {registry, username, password}`, allowlisted like `secrets` and, like it, never logged (`RunSpec.redact/1` exists but nothing calls it, so SI4 adds a test that no spec reaches a log). The agent writes a containers auth file (`{"auths": {<host>: {"auth": …}}}`, 0600) into the run's tmpfs secrets directory, passes it as `--authfile`, never runs `podman login` (which would persist it), and deletes the file when the pull ends, however it ends. Podman keeps no credential with the image. It is the primary's `Registry.with_authfile/3` (`worker/image/registry.ex:182`) with a tmpfs root.
* **Better later.** A short-lived bearer the primary mints per pull, scoped `repository:<path>:pull`, would expose less, but podman takes a user and password in an auth file rather than a bare bearer, and not every registry mints scoped tokens for every credential type **[S4]**; it would ride the same `pull_auth` field.
* Anonymous pulls only from a registry the operator marked `public_ok`; a repository holding the `claude` layer should never be one (§21.3.2). `--tls-verify=false` only where the operator set `insecure` for that registry (`Registry.tls_flags/1`, `worker/image/registry.ex:187`).

**Cold node, slow link.** For this repo: the toolchain image 843 MB (464 MB of it the base, §7.3), the seed about 200 MB (`deps` 52 MB, `_build` 145 MB, observed above), the CLI layers about 295 MB (`claude` 291.6 MB, `arb.escript` 2.6 MB) plus Codex, not measured here **[S1]**: about 1.3 GB unpacked, less on the wire. So:

* a registry node gets a pull-sized prepare bound (10 minutes) in place of the build-sized 25 (`nodes/session.ex:96`);
* a pull that outlives a refusal **keeps going**, as a publish does, so the next run finds the node warm; the refused run goes local under `prefer_remote` and is held under `remote_only` (`Publisher.fallback/2`);
* `image.prefetch` warms candidates after each publish (§21.2.4), and placement ranks a node that already holds the repo's **last published** digest (`hb.images`) before one that must pull, among nodes of equal load (`nodes/placement.ex:533`). The run's own digest is not known yet at that point: placement runs before `Dispatch.image_gate/6` publishes or resolves it (`worker/dispatch.ex:2850-2856`).

**Offline.** A node that holds the digest runs without the registry (`podman image exists` on the digest reference, then `--pull=never`). A node that lacks it and cannot reach the registry refuses `image_unavailable{pull_failed}`. The readiness report gains one `registry` row per registry the primary names in `hello_ok.registries` (an unauthenticated `GET /v2/`: 200 or 401 means reachable), and a node that reports a repo's registry unreachable is not a candidate for that repo (the machine-node counterpart of `registry_covered?/2` for clusters, `nodes/placement.ex:485`). A node that cannot reach the primary gets no work at all, and the runs it holds keep running (§10.4). An air-gapped node stays out of scope (§7.3's `save`/`load` fallback remains unbuilt).

**GC on the node** (`NodeAgent.ImageGC`, new).

* **A ledger, and nothing outside it.** `<node_home>/images.json` lists every digest the agent pulled: when, for which repository, and when a run last used it. GC removes ledger entries only, so an image the node's owner pulled, or anything else in that podman store, is never touched.
* **Never removed:** a digest a live, preparing or retained run (§10.4 quiesce) uses, one `NodeAgent.Exec` still keeps a gate context for (`node_agent/exec.ex:38`), or one pulled or used in the last 24 h (which covers a worker between two opens of its run).
* **Kept:** the newest two digests per repository by last use (the current one and the one before the last CLI update, so a rollback or a hold does not re-pull).
* **When:** hourly, after each pull, and under disk pressure (graph root free below 15 % or 20 GiB), when the 24-h grace is dropped and only what a live, preparing, retained or gate run needs, plus the newest digest per repository, survives. Each removal is `podman rmi` of the digest reference, which also drops the layers no other image uses; never `podman image prune`, which would take the owner's own dangling images too. A seed layer's deps-cache entry goes when no kept image uses that seed layer.
* **Reported** in `hb.images` (count, bytes); the doctor warns when pressure GC cannot get back above the line.
* **Once, after the migration** (§21.6 step 6): the agent removes `<node_home>/files/` and `<node_home>/deps-seed/`. Its old `localhost/arbiter-dev/*` images are **listed** by the doctor, not removed by the agent: their tags are content hashes shared by every install on the host (a node can be enrolled to two), and `Image.prune/1` filters on a label that does not name an install (`worker/image.ex:620`, `worker/image.ex:678`), so the operator removes them on the node (`podman image rm` of the tags the doctor lists) once no agent there builds plans.

### 21.5 Placement, and the checks that replace per-host coupling

**Rules that disappear or change.**

| Today | Becomes | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| A remote run's CLI must be installed on the **primary**, which ships it file by file | baked in the image; only the publisher needs it (OD13 a), or nobody does (OD13 b) | `worker/container_spawn.ex:1143-1152` |
| A machine node builds the base, toolchain and seed itself (distro, Docker Hub and Hex egress) | it pulls a digest and needs only the registry | `node_agent/run.ex:714-743`, `node_agent/run.ex:661-698` |
| `:non_claude_provider`: only Claude is placed | a set of providers with a remote credential path: `[:claude]` now, `:codex` once §21.3.3 lands, and a node is a candidate for a Codex run only when it advertises `caps.credential_sync: ["codex"]`; `remote_provider/1` reads the same set | `nodes/placement.ex:230`, `nodes/placement.ex:285`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1004-1005` |
| `prebuilt_needs_registry`: a `prebuilt` repo runs on a cluster only | on any registry node, machines included | `worker/container_spawn.ex:1044` |
| Machine nodes are exempt from `registry_covered?/2` | still exempt from prefixes (the primary hands over the credential), but not a candidate for a repo whose registry the node reports unreachable | `nodes/placement.ex:485` |

**What stays** is everything about the run rather than the host: `:not_podman`, the checkout veto and `:no_private_clone`, `:local_work`, `:scoped_git_credential`, `worker.placement`, pins, labels, health and capacity, `netpol_unenforced` and `psa_unenforced`, and a cluster's `caps.registries`.

**agy.** Its binary could ride the CLI layer by §21.2.3's rule once it is known to be self-contained (agy-podman-backend.md §3, A1: unknown). Its credential cannot: the design of record is a per-identity podman **volume** holding a private keyring, single-writer through `max_concurrent: 1` (agy-podman-backend.md §4 and §5). A volume is state on one machine; moving it per run makes a copy, and a copy of a rotating Google refresh token is exactly what that design exists to avoid. So agy stays local-only, and its podman exclusion (`worker/sandbox.ex:85`) remains the separate sandbox question it is. If agy ever gets an injectable credential (the API-key alternative of agy-podman-backend.md §4.3), it joins by the same rule as qwen.

**Checks that replace the coupling.**

1. **Preflight on the primary** (`ContainerSpawn.preflight_image/3`, `worker/container_spawn.ex:1084`, from `Dispatch.image_gate/6`): after `ensure_ready/2`, the published image's labels must name the run's provider (`image_unavailable{provider_not_in_image, provider}`) and a runtime-contract version the target node supports. Both are reads of the publish result, and a refusal is a placement decision (local or a hold), never a spawn failure.
2. **On the node, after the pull** (§21.2.1): `arbiter.runtime-contract` is in the agent's `caps.runtime_contracts` (`[1]` today), `arbiter.cli-set` is the spec's, and the provider's CLI labels are present; otherwise `refuse{image_unavailable, contract_mismatch, <item>}`. A `prebuilt` image built `FROM` the published base inherits the contract label, so this repeats where the image runs the check `Prebuilt` makes at plan time (`worker/image/prebuilt.ex:79`).
3. **Readiness** (`hello.readiness`): the `registry` reachability rows (§21.4), and the tmpfs a Codex run needs (`Secrets` already refuses without one).
4. **Doctor** (`arb server doctor`, nodes section, `apps/arbiter_cli/lib/arbiter_cli/cmd/doctor/checks.ex:2011`): per node `image source` (`registry`, or `build` with a "legacy path" warning during the window); per repo `image <repo>`: the digest, the CLI set and its versions, the providers it covers and the seed status; per registry: reachability (exists), anonymous read (extended to `nodes.registry`) and whether a pull credential is configured.

### 21.6 Migration, rollout, and what happens to `/nodes/files`

Each step ships on its own and leaves every existing path working:

1. **CLI set and layer** (SI1–SI3): `Worker.CliSet` behind `cli_mounts/2`, the file shelf and the layer; Codex in the layer; labels, snapshots and the hold; the seed → CLI order with deterministic builds; run provenance. Clusters pick it up at their next publish; nothing else changes.
2. **Machine-node pull, dual path** (SI4, then SI4a): agents advertise `caps.image_sources: ["registry", "build"]`; the primary sends a pinned ref without `cli` mounts when the node can pull and the repo has a registry with a pull credential the node may hold (§21.4), and the plan and shelf exactly as today otherwise. A setting `nodes.machine_image_source` (`auto` | `build`) defaults to `build` when SI4 ships, so the pull path is opt-in, and flips to `auto` with SI4a (the registry readiness rows and filter, the refusal mapping and the doctor rows). SI12 lands no later than this step, since machine nodes start pulling CLI and seed layers from `nodes.registry` here.
3. **Deps from the seed layer, GC, prefetch** (SI5–SI7).
4. **Codex on machine nodes** (SI8, SI9, SI9a, then SI11 opens placement to Codex on nodes with `caps.credential_sync`). The real-hardware acceptance is a Codex review run on oryx whose rotation lands back in the primary's login.
5. **Codex in pods** (SI10).
6. **Retire the legacy path** (SI13), one release after no node reported `build` (the doctor row of §21.5 shows it): plan delivery to nodes and the agent's `Image.Builder` child (`node_agent/supervisor.ex:121`), `nodes.machine_image_source`, the CLI shelf (`Nodes.Files`, `NodeAgent.Files`), the `cli` mount kind (`node_agent/run_spec.ex:74`, `node_agent/k8s/pod_spec.ex:353`), `remote_cli/2`, and the node cleanup of §21.4.
7. **Docs and runbook** (SI14).

**`/nodes/files/<sha256>`** has nothing left to serve after step 6. Its shelf half (`send_shelved/2`, `apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_controller.ex:401`) goes with the shelf, and its other half, the agent tarball by hash (`apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/controllers/node_controller.ex:381`), has no consumer in the tree: the join script and `NodeAgent.Upgrade` fetch `/nodes/agent/<version>.tar.gz` (`nodes/join_script.sh.eex:538`, `node_agent/upgrade.ex:133`). So the route (`apps/arbiter_web/lib/arbiter_web/router.ex:101`), its tests (`apps/arbiter_web/test/arbiter_web/node_join_test.exs:303`) and the `/nodes/files` column of §15.1 go too. If an external tool turns out to use the tarball alias, keeping that half is a one-line change; nothing in this design needs it.

**Skew.** Agents run the primary's exact version (§6), so each step reaches a node with the deploy that ships it. New behaviour is gated by `caps` (`image_sources`, `image_prefetch`, `credential_sync`, `runtime_contracts`), not by `proto`, which stays 1 (`node_agent/config.ex:74`): an `outdated` agent is never sent a field it does not know, and the bootstrap subset is untouched.

**Back-out.** Before step 6, `nodes.machine_image_source: build` restores the old machine path, and the remote-provider set back at `[:claude]` keeps Codex local. After step 6, redeploying the previous release does it, and the agents follow (§6).

### 21.7 What changes in the security model (§15.5)

* A compromised machine node can additionally read, for as long as a pull credential it kept stays valid, the repositories that credential reaches. §21.4's reach rule limits that to repositories holding runs the node may take anyway (the CLIs and dependency sources it is given to run, and older digests of them); a token broader than its registry path (H13) would reach more, which is why the rule is about what the token can read, not what it was handed for. It cannot push an image: it never sees a push credential.
* It holds a Codex refresh token while it runs a Codex run (§21.3.3).
* It can forge an `auth.json` upload; the primary adopts only a same-account, strictly newer, not future-dated login, so the worst it can do is break the login it was handed, which it could already do (AuthSync's accepted residue).
* Integrity improves for honest nodes, not against a compromised one: every node runs the bits the primary published and checked, by digest, instead of its own build of a plan (two builds of one tag can differ, and each node's mirrors and Docker Hub pulls were part of the trust base). A compromised node can still run whatever it likes on its own machine; its output stays untrusted, as before (§9).

### 21.8 Open decisions for the operator (OD11–OD14)

Each has the options, a recommendation and what waits on it.

**OD11. Local runs: keep the fast path or converge?** *(a) Keep it, with one resolver and recorded sets (recommended, §21.2.2).* (b) An opt-in `worker.image.local: published` for bit-for-bit parity. (c) Always converge. Blocks nothing in v1; (b) is a later ticket.

**OD12. Which Codex login do remote runs use?** *(a) The operator's, through the mirror (recommended for v1; the risks are §21.3.3's three residues).* (b) A dedicated login for remote runs: per-account Codex source homes, and placement keeps remote Codex runs on that account. (c) Access-token-only delivery, after a spike (S5). Needed from the operator: is a second ChatGPT account for remote runs acceptable? Blocks: SI11's switch for Codex.

**OD13. Where do the CLI bytes come from?** *(a) The primary's installed CLIs, snapshotted per set, with a hold (recommended).* (b) Pinned upstream releases the primary downloads and verifies by sha256: a fetcher per vendor, the version becomes a setting, and the primary no longer needs Codex installed. Blocks: SI1 and SI2 assume (a). **Decided 2026-10-26: (b).** `Worker.CliFetcher` (SI1a, bd-3g7qg0) fetches claude and codex from the npm registry at the versions pinned in `worker.cli.versions` (codex's `codex-resources/voice/` host excluded) and `arb` from the running release's GitHub asset, each checked against npm's `dist.integrity` or the release's `arb.sha256` before it enters the content-addressed `<image root>/cli-cache`. A daily job (`CliFetcher.Scheduler`, plus on demand) bumps each pin to the newest stable upstream version and appends a `cli_version_bumped` row; `worker.cli_set.hold` freezes it and `worker.cli.manual` opts a CLI out. Only the fetcher's own namespace can reach the network: publish and run paths read the cache (`CliFetcher.Cache`) and nothing else. `arb server doctor` reports the pins under `cli_versions`.

**OD14. CLI as layers or as a shared image volume?** *(a) Layers (recommended).* (b) An image volume once it is generally available on every cluster target (S3). Blocks nothing in v1.

| # | Title | Scope | Diff. | Why that rating | depends_on |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SI0 | **Spike: machine-node pull and Codex from an image layer, on oryx** | (a) `podman pull --authfile <tmpfs file>` of a digest reference with a read-only token: nothing left in podman's auth files or the image; (b) `podman build --timestamp` gives identical blobs for identical CLI files, the registry skips them on push, a node pulls only new blobs (S2); (c) the Codex ELF, `rg`, `bwrap` and siblings run from `/opt/arbiter/cli` in a layer under `--read-only --userns=keep-id`, and their size (S1); (d) how Codex rewrites `auth.json` inside a bind-mounted tmpfs `CODEX_HOME`, and a disk directory bound at its `sessions/` (S6, S11); (e) the per-pull token question (S4). Raw outputs in `docs/design/k8s-spike/`'s layout | D2 | probes on one real node and one registry, no product code | none |
| SI1 | **CLI set: one resolver for the bind mounts, the shelf and the layers; Codex in the image** | `Worker.CliSet` (resolve, content hash, snapshot to `<image root>/cli-sets/<set8>/`, manifest); `cli_mounts/2`, `remote_cli/2` and `Pipeline.cli_files/1` read it; one `COPY` per CLI in churn order above the seed, `--timestamp`; labels `arbiter.cli-set`, `arbiter.cli-providers`, `arbiter.cli.<name>.*`; the new tag scheme; the publish cache keyed on the set hash | D3 | reorders every repo's published layer graph and tag scheme while digests already handed to pods must stay valid, and changes local and remote CLI provisioning together | SI0 (b, c) |
| SI2 | **CLI-set hold and eager republish** | `worker.cli_set.hold` (installation setting); the publisher builds from a held snapshot; the `CliSet` watcher (at boot and every 10 min) republishes the repos that published in the last 14 days | D2 | a setting and a periodic task over SI1's snapshots | SI1 |
| SI3 | **Run provenance: image digest and CLI set on the run; Codex harness version from the label** | `worker_runs.image_ref`, `worker_runs.cli_set` (migration); written by local and remote spawns; `harness_version` from `arbiter.cli.<provider>.version` for container and remote spawns; run page and `arb worker show` | D2 | one migration, two writers and a read in G18 | SI1 |
| SI4 | **Machine nodes pull the published image** | agent: `caps.image_sources`, the pull clause in `default_image/2` with a tmpfs auth file, the label checks, `caps.runtime_contracts`; primary: `pull_credentials_ref` and `nodes.registry_pull_*` (validated, Cloak, never the push credential), the reach rule of §21.4, `pull_auth` in the spec (allowlisted, never logged, with a test), the per-run choice of source replacing `registry_node?/1`, no `cli` mounts for registry runs, `prebuilt_node/3` admits machines, `nodes.machine_image_source` (default `build`), the pull-sized prepare bound; e2e with the real agent and a stand-in registry | D3 | protocol, agent, configuration and credential handling on both sides; security-sensitive, though each piece copies an existing pattern | SI0 (a), SI1, SI12 |
| SI4a | **Pull-path readiness, refusals and doctor rows; the pull path on by default** | readiness `registry` rows from `hello_ok.registries` and the unreachable-registry candidate filter; the node's `contract_mismatch` and `pull_failed` refusals mapped to local or a hold; doctor rows per node (`image source`) and per registry (pull credential configured, its reach); `nodes.machine_image_source` default flips to `auto` | D2 | readiness, a placement filter and doctor rows on top of SI4's mechanism | SI4 |
| SI5 | **Deps from the image's seed layer, relocated, on nodes and in pods** | the seed layer ships `/opt/arbiter/seed/.complete` (lock, toolchain tag, `src`); the agent extracts it once per seed layer and installs with `DepsCache.install/3` (reflink and relocation) when the checkout's lock matches, else falls back to today's seed job; the pod `seed` script relocates too (a shell port of `DepsRelocate`, or the stamp read by a relocation step); tests with a NIF dependency through the offline gate | D3 | two runtimes (agent and pod script) and the relocation that the offline gate depends on; a wrong relocation is a silent full rebuild | SI4 |
| SI6 | **Node image GC** | `NodeAgent.ImageGC`: the ledger, the keep rules (live, preparing, retained, `Exec` gate contexts, 24 h, newest two per repository), `podman rmi` only (no `prune`), the pressure trigger, `hb.images`, the doctor warning; deps-cache entries go with their seed layer; the doctor lists legacy `localhost/arbiter-dev/*` images for the operator | D2 | contained to the agent, but it deletes on a machine the operator owns, so its safety rules need tests | SI4, SI5 |

Not placed yet (no carry-back) | D3 | a second provider through the spec, the agent and the transcript path, with a credential file that must never touch a node's disk | SI1, SI4 |
| SI9 | **Codex carry-back, primary side: the credential grant and the upload route** | the per-run grant in `Nodes.Session` (made at assign, kept 30 min past exit, cancel, owner death or quiesce, re-made from the run row after a restart, dropped at collect); `PUT /nodes/runs/:run/credentials/codex` (node auth, the grant, 64 KiB, staging, `AuthSync.sync/2`); re-open `sync`/`pull` against the mirror; a teardown sync for remote runs; the Reaper on the mirror | D3 | it guards the operator's real login against being stranded, across every way a run ends, including the ones today's upload windows refuse | SI8 |
