| SI11 | **Placement and preflight: providers from the image, not the host** | the remote-provider set replaces `:non_claude_provider` (Codex gated per node on `caps.credential_sync`); the `provider_not_in_image` preflight; the doctor's per-repo `image <repo>` row (digest, CLI set and versions, providers, seed status); `PlacementTest` and `LocalCapacityKindsTest` updated | D2 | eligibility, a preflight read and a doctor row; the mechanisms land in SI1, SI4 and SI9a | SI1, SI4a, SI9a |
| SI12 | **No credential in a layer: prebuilt image scans and the install-wide anonymous probe** | for **every** `prebuilt` plan: scan the image config `Env` and history (`image_unavailable{secret_in_image}`, for every run); for `seed: image`, the file-name rules and `deps/*/.git/config` URLs with userinfo; run the primary's seed job with the image environment reduced to an allowlist; extend `ensure_private/3` to `nodes.registry` with `nodes.registry_public_ok`; the repo-CI contract text (build-secret mounts only) | D2 | checks on existing paths; the work is tuning out false positives | none |
| SI13 | **Retire build-from-plan for nodes, the CLI shelf and `/nodes/files`** | §21.6 step 6: plan delivery, the agent's builder child (machine agents now advertise `caps.image = "registry"`), `nodes.machine_image_source`, `Nodes.Files`, `NodeAgent.Files`, the `cli` mount kind (`RunSpec`, `PodSpec`), `remote_cli/2`, `NodeController.file/2` with its route and tests; the one-time node cleanup; §15.1's table | D2 | deletions behind a path already off for a release, covered by guard tests | SI4a, SI5, SI6, SI11, and one release with no `build` node |
| SI14 | **Docs and runbook** | `docs/remote-workers-runbook.md` (node egress, pull credentials and their reach, Codex on nodes, GC), the k8s runbook (Codex), `podman-worker-containers.md` §4.2 (rotation at a distance), this section marked as built | D1 | text only | SI4a, SI9a, SI10, SI13 |

**Not filed until an OD says so:** OD11 (b), local parity opt-in (D2); OD12 (b), per-account Codex source homes (D3); OD12 (c), the access-token-only spike (D2); OD13 (b), an upstream CLI fetcher (D3); OD14 (b), an image-volume CLI (D3); a cluster pre-pull (D2).

**Order:** {SI0, SI12} → SI1 → {SI2, SI3, SI4} → {SI4a, SI5, SI8} → {SI6, SI9} → {SI7, SI9a} → {SI10, SI11} → SI13 → SI14. SI0 and SI12 depend on nothing and wait on no open decision, so they can start at once; the rest assume the recommendations of OD11–OD14 unless the operator decides otherwise.

### 21.10 Unverified assumptions (S-series; H1–H15, K1–K26 and U1–U20 still apply)

Nothing in §21 was run against a registry, a node's podman or the Codex CLI; the observations at the top are this run's own container. SI0 settles most of these on oryx.

| ID | Assumption | If it fails | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | The Codex vendored binary with its helpers is of the order of 100 MB | the churn numbers of §21.2.4 change; the design does not | SI0 |
| S2 | `podman build --timestamp <fixed>` writes identical layer blobs for identical files; a push skips a blob the registry has (and mounts it across repositories of one registry); a node pulling an image whose lower layers it holds fetches only the new blobs (containers/storage reuses layers by content) | per-CLI layers still work, but a Claude update also re-pushes and re-pulls the layer above it (`arb`), and the `claude` blob moves once per repo rather than once per registry and node | SI0 |
| S3 | Kubernetes image volumes are not generally available on the target clusters | OD14 (b) becomes cheap | operator (`kubectl version`, feature gates) |
| S4 | podman cannot take a bare registry bearer token in an auth file, and per-pull scoped tokens are not available across GHCR and GitLab for every credential type | `pull_auth` carries a short-lived token instead of the read credential | SI0 |
| S5 | The Codex CLI fails (rather than finishing) when its access token expires and it has no refresh token | OD12 (c) becomes viable | the OD12 (c) spike |
| S6 | The Codex CLI writes `auth.json` inside `CODEX_HOME` (in place, or by a rename within that directory), so a directory on tmpfs catches every write | mount differently (a single-file bind would break a rename) | SI0 |
| S7 | A node's `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` holds the small files of a Codex run's `CODEX_HOME` for every concurrent run (3.1 GiB on oryx, observed; systemd's default is 10 % of RAM) | refuse Codex runs on that node (`unschedulable`), as `Secrets` refuses without a tmpfs | SI8 |
| S8 | A ChatGPT login refreshes rarely enough (days) that a 5-s poll window is negligible | poll faster, or upload on the CLI's own refresh event if it reports one | SI9 telemetry |
| S9 | A Codex run in a pod works in the policy modes podman uses locally (the CLI's own `bwrap` sandbox may not nest under `RuntimeDefault` seccomp in a user namespace) | pods run Codex only in the modes that do not need `bwrap` | SI10 |
| S10 | The primary's seed job can run with a `prebuilt` image's environment cleared to an allowlist (podman's `--unsetenv-all` plus explicit `PATH`, `LANG`, `MIX_ENV`, `HEX_HOME`, `MIX_HOME`) without breaking the image's toolchain | pass an explicit `--env` override for each name the image sets instead | SI12 |
| S11 | podman honours a bind mount of a disk directory at `<CODEX_HOME>/sessions` nested inside the tmpfs-backed `CODEX_HOME` bind, and a pod can mount a `work` subpath inside a memory `emptyDir` mount | keep `sessions/` on tmpfs within the run's memory cap and upload it before the directory goes | SI0, SI10 |
